# The merged statements

Historical, written 2026-11-22: every statement of value the blind readers quoted from the owner's messages, in the messages' date order, exact repeats of a quote under the same label removed and nothing else. 1715 statements. Each links to the message it was quoted from (a record's turn, a transcript's section, or a Copilot-era file and the line its message starts on); which readers quoted it follows the link; "not verbatim" marks a quote not found word for word in that message. The method and the comparison are in [the README](README.md).

- **S0001** [2025-10-16:1](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 1 · sonnet: “My desire is for Github Copilot, while working in agent mode, to be given intuitive clues about salient context sitting \_just outside\_ the bounds of its current windowed knowledge, \_based on the changes it is making\_.”
- **S0002** [2025-10-16:8](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 287 · sonnet: “It is very important to me that we do the least amount of work possible and leverage solved problems (especially officially solved problems that are continuously improving with enormous labor and backing behind them).”
- **S0003** [2025-10-16:23](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 791 · sonnet: “Imperfect AST may be traded off for the ability to see implications beyond what a single compiler ever could”
- **S0004** [2025-10-16:25](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 1005 · sonnet: “But having forced frontmatter or forced command line calls sounds absolutely terrible.”
- **S0005** [2025-10-16:25](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 1005 · sonnet: “The graph should be something that can easily be rebuilt fresh if deleted, just like a VS Code workspace index.”
- **S0006** [2025-10-16:28](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 1147 · sonnet: “We are aiming to build this out of common sense and do the very least amount of wheel reinvention possible, finding places to rely on solved problems and absorb their improvements as our own rather than compete to keep up with their improvements.”
- **S0007** [2025-10-16:31](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 1217 · sonnet: “We do not need a great deal of pomp and circumstance.”
- **S0008** [2025-10-16:34](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 1267 · sonnet: “I think that this "degraded-mode" behavior should be the default behavior. It is a little sugar on top if we have the support of AST for our task of building the knowledge graph of the code, but it is \*\*not\*\* required. Our process should be capable of building it out on its own.”
- **S0009** [2025-10-16:34](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 1267 · sonnet: “The aim is to be reproducible. The artifact must be very common-sense.”
- **S0010** [2025-10-16:40](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 1502 · sonnet: “which as I've said before, is primarily riding on VS Code improvements and common sense additions”
- **S0011** [2025-10-16:44](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 1795 · sonnet: “I am charged by the user prompt, not by the token or LLM action. This is to encourage high quality outputs from Github Copilot.”
- **S0012** [2025-10-16:48](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 2337 · sonnet: “Try to keep in mind what you need to ask me, the developer-user to do for testing and what we need the typical user to perform for setup, striving to keep the barrier to entry low in both fronts.”
- **S0013** [2025-10-16:50](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 2481 · sonnet: “We want to get the general shape on paper first, knowing that it won't be \_perfectly\_ right.”
- **S0014** [2025-10-16:53](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 2636 · sonnet: “I would prefer whatever is genuinely most portable.”
- **S0015** [2025-10-16:54](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 2655 · sonnet: “We want to avoid reinventing the wheel during agentic coding. Software built by LLM-driven means is necessarily built of common sense. What we build must be that.”
- **S0016** [2025-10-16:56](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 2691 · sonnet: “I want to keep the focus on the actual implementation of our vision.”
- **S0017** [2025-10-16:60](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 2964 · sonnet: “I like linting that enforces programmatic correctness, but dislike linting which forces formatting conventions or is otherwise pedantic.”
- **S0018** [2025-10-16:65](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-16/2025-10-16.md) line 3625 · sonnet: “That means copilot-instructions needs to only pertain itself with the outermost shell of concerns: things which will be forever true; instructions for how to handle quirks in the workspace; layouts of the directories; all as terse as possible.”
- **S0019** [2025-10-17:6](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-17/2025-10-17.md) line 140 · sonnet: “You'll see me press hard on this because these summary documents become a source of truth quite quickly. Little imperfections can ripple through future understandings. It's worth pressing to get right.”
- **S0020** [2025-10-17:16](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-17/2025-10-17.md) line 1065 · sonnet: “designing for ease of interpretation by Github Copilot”
- **S0021** [2025-10-19:1](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-19/2025-10-19.md) line 1 · sonnet: “The idea is to make these summaries into places of centralized truth where you can see clearly and easily where files and architectural decisions came from.”
- **S0022** [2025-10-19:3](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-19/2025-10-19.md) line 239 · sonnet: “We should be thinking about and prioritizing dogfooding this as early as possible, tuning it yourself to meet your own contextual needs.”
- **S0023** [2025-10-19:6](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-19/2025-10-19.md) line 809 · sonnet: “we should also prioritize finding ways to run our software's pieces on our own codebase to verify its outputs, rather than launching a new VS Code window.”
- **S0024** [2025-10-19:9](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-19/2025-10-19.md) line 1150 · sonnet: “I must strongly strongly encourage developing a way to test the components of our software here against our software, in our workspace.”
- **S0025** [2025-10-19:16](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-19/2025-10-19.md) line 2009 · sonnet: “We do not need to spend a dime on copilot requests during our tests”
- **S0026** [2025-10-19:19](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-19/2025-10-19.md) line 2140 · sonnet: “For any given change in any given file, this extension provides the definitive answer to the question: "What files will be impacted by this change?"”
- **S0027** [2025-10-19:19](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-19/2025-10-19.md) line 2140 · sonnet: “I think it would be much more wise for us to build up integration test cases of higher and higher complexity, some providing AST support and some without; gradually increasing in file count and cross-reference complexity.”
- **S0028** [2025-10-19:19](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-19/2025-10-19.md) line 2140 · sonnet: “Once we are really really satisfied with our ability to detect the true set of perturbances for any given change \_on code files\_, \_then\_ we work our way up to code-to-docs and docs-to-docs.”
- **S0029** [2025-10-19:19](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-19/2025-10-19.md) line 2140 · sonnet: “This is ultimately the artifact that we are seeking, and the artifact which, when queried properly, can definitively give us the ability to forecast the ripples brought about by any change in any file in the workspace.”

- **S0058** [2025-10-24:5](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 126 · opus: “These summaries provide us an enormous amount of grounding relative to our commit history.”
- **S0059** [2025-10-24:8](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 329 · opus: “As I mentioned yesterday, a way that I often tackle a big implementation task is by doing it MDMD docs-first.”
- **S0060** [2025-10-24:8](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 329 · opus: “I write up the layer 4 (and sometimes layer 3) MDMD docs that I \_think\_ I will need, and then implement against those, allowing the harsh realities I discover during implementation to be reflected back into the docs, and then from the docs back to the implementation, so on and so forth until the two settle in harmony with each other, mutually described and mutually correct.”
- **S0061** [2025-10-24:8](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 329 · opus, sonnet: “This approach is exceptionally useful for LLM-assisted development because it is profoundly durable across the autosummarization windows.”
- **S0062** [2025-10-24:8](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 329 · opus, sonnet: “Those MDMD files tend to be linking hubs and allow easy, navigable-from-any-entry-point access to an up-to-date web of truth about our project.”

- **S0095** [2025-10-24:55](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 8179 · opus: “And if there must be an architecture to these tests, then there must be implementations of them.”

- **S0098** [2025-10-24:55](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 8179 · sonnet: “I think that layer 3 entries should exist for our testing architecture: an architecture for unit testing, an architecture for integration testing, and an architecture for other forms of testing (like the \`npm run graph:snapshot -- --quiet\` and \`npm run graph:audit\` command pair as our first dogfooding tool!).”
- **S0099** [2025-10-24:55](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 8179 · sonnet: “If a test's SUT can cleanly be mapped to one or a small handful of existing layer 4 MDMD files, link to the test file from the layer 4 MDMD for the unit(s) it tests. The best candidate (test/test infrastructure) files for being given their own layer 4 MDMD file are ones which \_consume/interact with/test\_ more than one other code file.”
- **S0100** [2025-10-24:56](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 8302 · opus: “A little odd in my opinion to have MDMD of bespoke project-only scripts, but at the same time, I think that we could have a (layer 3) architecture file that links to several (layer 4) implementation MDMD files for the workspace scripts that we use regularly.”
- **S0101** [2025-10-24:56](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 8302 · opus: “One thing I want you to try and remember is that every Layer 4 MDMD doc must capably answer the question: \*\*Why does this file exist\*\*?”
- **S0102** [2025-10-24:56](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 8302 · opus: “If we think of code not as an explicit asset but as a liability, code must justify itself.”
- **S0103** [2025-10-24:56](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 8302 · opus: “You won't need to use it all the time (plenty is common sense in what we're doing; indeed that's what LLM-driven coding is best at), but there will be times where giving your future clean-slated self more context can be really useful.”
- **S0104** [2025-10-24:56](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 8302 · opus: “A web of truth is what we wish to create in the MDMD docs.”
- **S0105** [2025-10-24:56](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 8302 · opus: “One that is easily navigable from any entry point.”
- **S0106** [2025-10-24:56](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 8302 · sonnet: “One thing I want you to try and remember is that every Layer 4 MDMD doc must capably answer the question: \*\*Why does this file exist\*\*? If we think of code not as an explicit asset but as a liability, code must justify itself.”
- **S0107** [2025-10-24:56](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 8302 · sonnet: “A web of truth is what we wish to create in the MDMD docs. One that is easily navigable from any entry point.”
- **S0108** [2025-10-24:57](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 8363 · opus: “This is to encourage high agency and high-quality responses.”
- **S0109** [2025-10-24:57](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 8363 · sonnet: “The cost model of github copilot is such that the user is charged per prompt, not per token or per request. This is to encourage high agency and high-quality responses.”
- **S0110** [2025-10-24:59](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 8641 · opus, sonnet: “Again, we want to minimize the orphan links/docs within reason.”
- **S0111** [2025-10-24:61](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-24/2025-10-24.md) line 8989 · opus, sonnet: “(We do commit chat history as an auditability artifact; we will find more uses for it as we move forward as well).”
- **S0112** [2025-10-25:4](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 564 · opus: “Often I like to begin development with something of a specific workspace-based \*\*challenge\*\*: something incredibly concrete that we can work toward and incidentally tackle our goals as we do so.”
- **S0113** [2025-10-25:4](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 564 · opus, sonnet: “Broken markdown links are a telltale sign of LLM hallucinations: a link to a file that does not exist.”
- **S0114** [2025-10-25:4](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 564 · opus: “This would give our tooling quite the workout \*\*and\*\* capably be part of a suite that I'm tentatively calling "SlopCop" (kinda like that old StyleCop linter package name), a set of tools built to catch, in linting-like ways, the very strange modern problems that have arisen in the age of LLM-assisted development.”
- **S0115** [2025-10-25:4](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 564 · sonnet: “a suite that I'm tentatively calling "SlopCop" (kinda like that old StyleCop linter package name), a set of tools built to catch, in linting-like ways, the very strange modern problems that have arisen in the age of LLM-assisted development.”
- **S0116** [2025-10-25:6](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 649 · opus, sonnet: “As I mentioned yesterday and as I'll mention again today, I tend to do development \*\*docs-first\*\*.”
- **S0117** [2025-10-25:6](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 649 · opus: “I start from MDMD, designing what I \_think\_ I'll need, and then I implement it and allow harsh reality to slam back against my best intentions, reflecting back into the docs, which then reflect back down to the implementation, over and over and over until the two are in perfect sync with each other, settled.”
- **S0118** [2025-10-25:6](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 649 · opus: “This process I call "Code Like Clay", as it is quite similar to spinning out a pot on a pottery wheel.”
- **S0119** [2025-10-25:6](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 649 · opus: “In terms of LLM-driven development, "Code Like Clay" is astoundingly powerful.”
- **S0120** [2025-10-25:6](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 649 · opus: “This is explicitly to encourage and incentivize high-quality responses and high-agency behavior in github copilot.”
- **S0121** [2025-10-25:6](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 649 · opus: “You are of course highly encouraged to ask for my help for clarifications and guidance, but if your aim is to simply pop back in and say "I just finished XYZ microtask", please just continue through.”
- **S0122** [2025-10-25:6](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 649 · opus, sonnet: “(It also makes for better training data to further improve Github Copilot's abilities; traces in which copilot elegantly weaved 150 continuous actions into a completely stable green build with an enhancement completed before considering the cumulative agentic response completed are considered gold).”
- **S0123** [2025-10-25:6](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 649 · sonnet: “This process I call "Code Like Clay", as it is quite similar to spinning out a pot on a pottery wheel. In terms of LLM-driven development, "Code Like Clay" is astoundingly powerful.”
- **S0124** [2025-10-25:6](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 649 · sonnet · not verbatim: “the cost model of Github Copilot in VS Code is such that I pay for every user prompt, not every token or tool call or every request. This is explicitly to encourage and incentivize high-quality responses and high-agency behavior in github copilot.”
- **S0125** [2025-10-25:6](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 649 · sonnet: “if your aim is to simply pop back in and say "I just finished XYZ microtask", please just continue through.”
- **S0126** [2025-10-25:7](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 792 · opus: “I perhaps should have reserved speaking the name until later -- LLMs do get attached to new pharase coinage very quickly for some reason -- I don't want to give the impression that the improvements we have been experimenting with in this workspace are somehow totally and absolutely bespoke and separate things.”
- **S0127** [2025-10-25:7](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 792 · opus: “I think that the path to true workspace-wide pseudocode AST has several wins along the way.”
- **S0128** [2025-10-25:7](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 792 · opus: “One of the simple wins is the ability to detect broken links and orphans in markdown files.”
- **S0129** [2025-10-25:7](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 792 · opus: “The next step of that would be the ability to detect broken links and orphans in code-like files which use paths in them (i.e. HTML/CSS files that mention an image file path).”
- **S0130** [2025-10-25:7](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 792 · opus: “The next step after that would be the ability to detect broken links and orphans in \*\*SYMBOLS\*\* from code files.”
- **S0131** [2025-10-25:7](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 792 · opus: “What's particularly cool about our MDMD approach is that, so long as we have really good verification of markdown links (including the presence or absence of a header that is being specifically directed to \_in\_ a markdown file), we have a really good \_proxy\_ for the completed product.”
- **S0132** [2025-10-25:7](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 792 · opus: “When we build our code off of that \_proxy\_, MDMD, we hopefully build up the \*\*actual\*\* full functionality.”
- **S0133** [2025-10-25:7](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 792 · opus: “Slopcop is an early quick win in which we try to detect broken markdown links.”
- **S0134** [2025-10-25:7](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 792 · sonnet: “I don't want to give the impression that the improvements we have been experimenting with in this workspace are somehow totally and absolutely bespoke and separate things. I think that the path to true workspace-wide pseudocode AST has several wins along the way.”
- **S0135** [2025-10-25:7](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 792 · sonnet: “What's particularly cool about our MDMD approach is that, so long as we have really good verification of markdown links (including the presence or absence of a header that is being specifically directed to \_in\_ a markdown file), we have a really good \_proxy\_ for the completed product. When we build our code off of that \_proxy\_, MDMD, we hopefully build up the \*\*actual\*\* full functionality.”
- **S0136** [2025-10-25:7](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 792 · sonnet: “So, slopcop. Slopcop is an early quick win in which we try to detect broken markdown links. That is all.”
- **S0137** [2025-10-25:16](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 2298 · opus: “The additional time spent on unit tests is still absolutely nothing in comparison to the time we spend on integration tests.”
- **S0138** [2025-10-25:28](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 3851 · opus: “If you have design questions and decisions that should need a PM, please surface it to me, but you are the lead dev on this.”
- **S0139** [2025-10-25:32](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4202 · opus: “Our absence of CICD pipelines has primarily been about velocity.”
- **S0140** [2025-10-25:32](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4202 · opus: “It is only us developing this; we're trying to make it so Github Copilot can, through excellent specification, create a true enhancement on its own functionality.”
- **S0141** [2025-10-25:32](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4202 · sonnet: “Our absence of CICD pipelines has primarily been about velocity. It is only us developing this; we're trying to make it so Github Copilot can, through excellent specification, create a true enhancement on its own functionality.”
- **S0142** [2025-10-25:33](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4230 · opus: “You are taking on the role of lead developer and I'm taking on the role of PM.”
- **S0143** [2025-10-25:33](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4230 · opus: “This is explicitly to encourage and incentivize both high quality responses \_and ever-increasing agency behavior in github copilot\_.”
- **S0144** [2025-10-25:33](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4230 · opus, sonnet: “In essence, the most "exciting" data for training appears to be the streaks in which Github Copilot goes out and performs 150 agentic steps before coming in for the final reply, having the entire set of work done and every test green.”
- **S0145** [2025-10-25:33](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4230 · opus: “That's the ideal, obviously (but actually has happened by your hands one or two times during development! Absolutely electrifying to watch when it was happening!).”
- **S0146** [2025-10-25:33](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4230 · opus: “We expect unexpecteds to come up.”
- **S0147** [2025-10-25:33](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4230 · opus: “We expect occasional holes in our vision/design -- things we didn't account for.”
- **S0148** [2025-10-25:33](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4230 · opus, sonnet: “Do your best to carry out what you've described in full, knowing that even if you shoot for the moon and miss, you'll still land among the stars.”
- **S0149** [2025-10-25:33](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4230 · sonnet: “The cost model of Github Copilot is such that I, the user, am charged for every user prompt -- not every token or every request. This is explicitly to encourage and incentivize both high quality responses \_and ever-increasing agency behavior in github copilot\_.”
- **S0150** [2025-10-25:37](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4530 · opus: “Then work your way into more and more difficult territory, noting that, as much as possible, we do not want to have to build any actual separate compilers, and wish to lean on solved problems and ergonomics where they exist (if they exist) with respect to gathering an AST for other formal languages.”
- **S0151** [2025-10-25:37](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4530 · opus: “Think about the kinds of questions and assurances our tooling should be capable of answering at the symbol level, and what kind of value it provides to end users \*\*and yourself, Github Copilot, while working on code\*\* (hence why we try to raise "lint" based on our findings when we're coming in through the actual VS Code extension route that our integration tests go through).”
- **S0152** [2025-10-25:37](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4530 · sonnet: “as much as possible, we do not want to have to build any actual separate compilers, and wish to lean on solved problems and ergonomics where they exist (if they exist) with respect to gathering an AST for other formal languages.”
- **S0153** [2025-10-25:39](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4633 · opus, sonnet: “Optimize for VSCode/Github compatibility on slug format.”
- **S0154** [2025-10-25:39](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4633 · opus, sonnet: “On the other hand, I kind of want to run the experiment of enforcing this constraint, because I suspect that it will actually supercharge the power of MDMD.”
- **S0155** [2025-10-25:39](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4633 · opus: “It will be a pain in the @$$ getting the lint to zero, but it will also incentivize... get this.... \*\*downsizing\*\*!”
- **S0156** [2025-10-25:39](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4633 · opus, sonnet: “If docs are code, and \_excess\_ code is a liability rather than an asset, we must therefore conclude that \_excess\_ documentation is a liability rather than an asset.”
- **S0157** [2025-10-25:39](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4633 · opus: “The places we can take this idea of symbol-level-provenance in markdown are rather astounding and profound.”
- **S0158** [2025-10-25:39](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4633 · opus: “Imagine that once you have performed, in an AST-guardrailed way, an entire software design in pure pseudocode documentation from vision to implementation plan, and you can then send out 3 different AI agents to implement that in 3 different programming languages, or with 3 different approaches?”
- **S0159** [2025-10-25:39](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4633 · opus, sonnet: “The promise of docs-as-code is big, so if we can make the markdown symbol-level intelligence work, that would be fabulous.”
- **S0160** [2025-10-25:39](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 4633 · opus: “I would appreciate if you could keep those spec-kit docs up to date too, but if you think that we've outgrown them (they were used to bootstrap this workspace and development effort), I could understand that too.”
- **S0161** [2025-10-25:47](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 6073 · sonnet: “Just note that the copilot-instructions file is to hold things that will be "forever true" across the workspace, in any file or any action; it's an important file to keep accurate and right-sized”
- **S0162** [2025-10-25:48](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-25/2025-10-25.md) line 6130 · sonnet: “I think that we should be building this to work with as much standard markdown as possible, \_while also refining our own MDMD approach and tooling against each other iteratively as needed\_. ("Code like clay", remember?)”
- **S0163** [2025-10-26:7](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-26/2025-10-26.md) line 1099 · sonnet: “I think that, for MDMD, we should be more comfortable being flexible and allowing renames of headings.”
- **S0164** [2025-10-26:7](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-26/2025-10-26.md) line 1099 · sonnet: “For test fixtures that we want to stand in as representative examples of typical markdown, I can definitely understand disabling symbol-level intelligence there.”
- **S0165** [2025-10-26:11](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-26/2025-10-26.md) line 1308 · sonnet: “This is why I have preserved the chat history -- for virtually any file in the workspace, we are able to determine where it came from and why.”
- **S0166** [2025-10-26:12](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-26/2025-10-26.md) line 1772 · sonnet: “I commit chat history as it is an incredibly useful artifact that continues to help us time and time again. I have yet to regret the decision to keep these as markdown files.”
- **S0167** [2025-10-26:12](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-26/2025-10-26.md) line 1772 · sonnet: “\*\*proper MDMD documentation of our changes must be performed first\*\*”
- **S0168** [2025-10-26:21](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-26/2025-10-26.md) line 3518 · sonnet: “The next big milestione I want us moving towards is this: "For every public symbol in our codebase, there must be at least one mention of it in an MDMD doc".”
- **S0169** [2025-10-26:21](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-26/2025-10-26.md) line 3518 · sonnet: “We can refine from there to further constrain (i.e. must be a layer 4 MDMD doc, must be a doc whose name corresponds to the name of the implementation class file, etc. etc.) as needed to get our "Code Like Clay" approach rigorous through-and-through.”
- **S0170** [2025-10-26:22](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-26/2025-10-26.md) line 3698 · sonnet: “One thing that I personally thing will be an upfront problem will be verifying the reverse: that no "symbol" in a layer 4 MDMD doc corresponds to an \*\*orphan\*\*.”
- **S0171** [2025-10-26:22](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-26/2025-10-26.md) line 3698 · sonnet: “Code Like Clay will help us battle test our ideas against what really exists.”
- **S0172** [2025-10-26:22](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-26/2025-10-26.md) line 3698 · sonnet: “I think we're going to be astounded by what this level of correctness on our design is going to provide.”
- **S0173** [2025-10-26:25](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-26/2025-10-26.md) line 3923 · sonnet: “I try to avoid having folks take my word for things, and rely a lot on capturing measurements and sharing them.”
- **S0174** [2025-10-26:28](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-26/2025-10-26.md) line 4923 · sonnet: “Here's one possibility for a longer-vision: bidirectionality in layer 4 MDMD docs could take the form of bidirectional replication to docstrings/other intellisense-able comment-like structures in the actual code files at the proper symbol locations, such that changing a code file's public surface (so long as the developer inserts a new docstring for the new programmatic surface) could be deterministically extracted to a specific section of each layer 4 MDMD doc.”
- **S0175** [2025-10-26:28](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-26/2025-10-26.md) line 4923 · sonnet: “I just want you considering the possibility of determinstic bridging between code docstrings and section(s) of MDMD doc(s) \*\*as you draft your changes, so that you can hopefully future-proof them for this possibility\*\*.”
- **S0176** [2025-10-26:29](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-26/2025-10-26.md) line 5168 · sonnet: “I think the bulleted list idea is going to be folly. I bet you're going to need to be able to exercise more markdown semantics freedom to get good docstrings out.”
- **S0177** [2025-10-27:11](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-27/2025-10-27.md) line 808 · sonnet: “Detecting orphans as part of our suit for verifying internal correctness would be a wonderful addition).”
- **S0178** [2025-10-27:14](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-27/2025-10-27.md) line 1208 · sonnet: “For the vast majority of MDMD docs, symbols should \*\*not\*\* be bullets in a bulleted list.”
- **S0179** [2025-10-27:14](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-27/2025-10-27.md) line 1208 · sonnet: “I explicitly asked that we abandon the approach of relegating symbols to bullets in lists, as it was unlikely to provide us with the flexibility that we would need to exercise all our formatting we would want if we ever built a 2-way docstring bridge between layer 4 MDMD and code files.”
- **S0180** [2025-10-27:15](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-27/2025-10-27.md) line 1374 · sonnet: “Don't use bulleted lists for symbols that you want to get bridged.”
- **S0181** [2025-10-27:27](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-27/2025-10-27.md) line 3273 · sonnet: “The overarching idea here is that we are going to improve our Layer 1 MDMD docs by cumulatively mining the knowledge of user intent that has already been spoken throughout our development history.”
- **S0182** [2025-10-27:29](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-27/2025-10-27.md) line 3420 · sonnet: “(The more you can go without user intervention, the better the training data you create).”
- **S0183** [2025-10-27:59](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-27/2025-10-27.md) line 5530 · sonnet: “We want to improve and crystallize our overall vision.”
- **S0184** [2025-10-27:59](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-27/2025-10-27.md) line 5530 · sonnet: “These docs you generate? They \*\*will\*\* become part of the overall programmatically-correct lattice of documentation that we're building.”
- **S0185** [2025-10-27:64](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-27/2025-10-27.md) line 6003 · sonnet: “I want to think that vision and requirements kind of "bubble up" out of our chats, and that's the overall exercise you're seeing me doing here.”
- **S0186** [2025-10-27:65](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-27/2025-10-27.md) line 6045 · sonnet: “Eh, that's a bit more of a layer 4 concern than a layer 1 concern. These operational concerns are very internal and very tangential to the external \_product-like thing\_ we want to create and share with the world in the form of a VS Code extension.”
- **S0187** [2025-10-27:65](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-27/2025-10-27.md) line 6045 · sonnet: “Ultimately, we're building tooling to benefit you in ways that make development crazy easy.”
- **S0188** [2025-10-27:65](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-27/2025-10-27.md) line 6045 · sonnet: “I think that, at the absolute "nirvana" point of our tooling/extension, a person could start with a single prompt describing what they want to create, and just by linter rules bringing up necessity after necessity in the docs and code, the project just "builds itself" out of common sense and a noisy intelligent linter whining at copilot as it implements!”
- **S0189** [2025-10-27:65](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-27/2025-10-27.md) line 6045 · sonnet: “I want Github Copilot to be set up to fall into the pit of success by building a lattice of truth that is verifiable and auditable and capable of being entered and queried from anywhere in numerous different ways.”
- **S0190** [2025-10-27:71](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-27/2025-10-27.md) line 6379 · sonnet: “I say we tighten the simple workspace towards compliance and, if necessary, relax constraints or broaden detection patterns gently to make a reasonable set of markdown retain functionality (favoring more edits to markdown than edits to code) .”
- **S0191** [2025-10-28:9](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-28/2025-10-28.md) line 598 · sonnet: “You're coming at this quite fresh, so you'll be able to tell me pretty independenttly whether or not you feel like they're adding value to \_your\_ understanding. That is the only reason they exist.”
- **S0192** [2025-10-28:14](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-28/2025-10-28.md) line 747 · sonnet: “My question was more about me prying for \*\*simplifications\*\* that we might be able to make that would allow us, and our tooling, to work across a broader variety of workspaces or under fewer assumptions, but I'm okay to back down on that for now if your analysis of our MDMD docs indicates that we aren't carrying much in the way of technical debt/cruft.”
- **S0193** [2025-10-28:15](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-28/2025-10-28.md) line 1214 · sonnet: “I overall like the idea of consolidating docs which have duplication.”
- **S0194** [2025-10-28:34](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-28/2025-10-28.md) line 2830 · sonnet: “We check both of these artifacts in, as they are extremely useful for locating the circumstances around file originations/edits, and all kinds of other uses (many not yet explored!).”
- **S0195** [2025-10-28:36](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-28/2025-10-28.md) line 2977 · sonnet: “Don't leave docs out of date. If those tasks are done, mark them. If you're walking around the workspace and see something obviously out of place, please fix it.”
- **S0196** [2025-10-28:37](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-28/2025-10-28.md) line 2989 · sonnet: “please ensure that you follow a "Code Like Clay" approach, where you are continuously flowing back and forth between code and docs until the two are in harmony with each other.”
- **S0197** [2025-10-29:16](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 676 · sonnet: “You literally have LLM tools to check the VS Code API. There is no reason to guess on this whatsoever.”
- **S0198** [2025-10-29:18](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 749 · sonnet: “the longer you can go without user intervention (so long as you don't \_need\_ user/PM intervention/help), the better the training data you create for future Github Copilot versions (i.e. from user thumbs up/thumbs down-based RLHF).”
- **S0199** [2025-10-29:30](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 2369 · sonnet: “We need to figure out what the LLM is doing and saying rather than keep increasing timeouts.”
- **S0200** [2025-10-29:32](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 2450 · sonnet: “You're lead developer. I expect you to debug this. If there is a decision that you need a PM for (me), a true fork in the road decision or true impassable roadblock, I'm here for it, but if it's debugging the software you wrote? No, you should expect to do that debugging.”
- **S0201** [2025-10-29:45](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 4651 · sonnet: “What can we, purveyors of a putative VS Code extension, do as a means to provide that excellent Markdown intelligence \_when and where users want it\_, and \_according to their file paths and conventions\_?”
- **S0202** [2025-10-29:47](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 4716 · sonnet: “I feel like our current workspace is chock full of exemplary \_ingredients\_ and no cohesive central \_product\_, perhaps?”
- **S0203** [2025-10-29:48](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 4766 · sonnet: “I'm thinking more broadly about the possibility of documentation with live/hydrated components.”
- **S0204** [2025-10-29:49](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 4794 · sonnet: “Part of what I love about MDMD and the way that it all works (especially and including our chat history here in the workspace) is that \_the workspace is the entire universe\_! There is nothing salient to developing this software that we cannot get from inside this workspace!”
- **S0205** [2025-10-29:49](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 4794 · sonnet: “I wanted to know, either before or after making a set of changes, in any arbitrary workspace, \*\*what the workspace-wide consequences would be\*\*.”
- **S0206** [2025-10-29:49](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 4794 · sonnet: “We open ourselves up to the world wide web by default and we open ourselves up to trouble trouble trouble.”
- **S0207** [2025-10-29:50](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 4841 · sonnet: “The primary consumer of the outputs of our tooling are LLMs! I think that we need to put some very pioneering focus into returning our results in mutually-human-and-LLM-readable formats like Markdown charts or ASCII art diagrams which SHOW relationships and cause and effect.”
- **S0208** [2025-10-29:57](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 5548 · sonnet: “I think that the middle ground should be tiered adoption by users.”
- **S0209** [2025-10-29:57](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 5548 · sonnet: “At first, we provide you with a fair bit of intelligence (with some false positives/false negatives) but require \*\*no workspace/documentation contract changes\*\* at all.”
- **S0210** [2025-10-29:57](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 5548 · sonnet: “I want greenfield projects to fall down the pit of success, starting from a simple prompt and a set of code-and-docs rules that causes linting to drive copilot to inadvertently developing and documenting the entire thing, from pure common-sense.”
- **S0211** [2025-10-29:57](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 5548 · sonnet: “I want brownfield projects to get a new chance to have always-up-to-date documentation and documentation-based evidence of program correctness.”
- **S0212** [2025-10-29:57](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 5548 · sonnet: “I want brownfield projects to be able to have a good chunk of their documentation generated procedurally by our tooling if they so request it.”
- **S0213** [2025-10-29:57](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-29/2025-10-29.md) line 5548 · sonnet: “I want all projects to have total clarify on the effect of any arbitrary change on any other arbitrary workspace file, and with a focus on salient connections between files and symbols.”
- **S0214** [2025-10-30:5](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 246 · sonnet: “I believe that we can better decouple the specific structural ideas conventions of MDMD from their verification, such that the systems we expose to end users can be composed and configured to recreate MDMD correctness in the same way that we have.”
- **S0215** [2025-10-30:5](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 246 · sonnet: “I think that the major draw -- the major benefit -- that we've been gaining, correctness-wise, from layer 4 MDMD docs is this mandate that every code file receive its own layer 4 MDMD doc and every layer 4 MDMD doc expose its public symbols.”
- **S0216** [2025-10-30:5](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 246 · sonnet: “I guess what I'm trying to say is this: if we want to give people (including ourselves) the ability to create \*\*rules and expectations\*\* where things which can be denoted as files or symbols within a specific set of file paths/glob patterns, we would enable far more interesting combinations and permutations and explorations for people to find what really works for them.”
- **S0217** [2025-10-30:8](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 343 · sonnet: “Once we have documented conventions that we love, then we start turning those conventions into programmatically enforced conventions.”
- **S0218** [2025-10-30:10](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 378 · sonnet: “We are looking to improve the bottom more than the top.”
- **S0219** [2025-10-30:10](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 378 · sonnet: “Much of how I approach building a new process is to, myself, do it manually once or twice. Then I take that common sense I employed and automate it.”
- **S0220** [2025-10-30:11](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 412 · sonnet: “The structure of MDMD is very much built to help \_you\_, Github Copilot, understand a project completely from near-zero-context at a glance from nearly any angle. It exists to bring you into a structured lattice of internally-consistent truth. It also exists to enforce correctness: plans made are promises that must be kept lest you be left with annoying intelligent linter warnings”
- **S0221** [2025-10-30:13](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 455 · sonnet: “Like I said before, this system is designed to be maximally useful to \*\*you\*\*.”
- **S0222** [2025-10-30:15](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 600 · sonnet: “I would say, favor any \*\*symbol\*\* being a markdown HEADER (H1-H6) so that it can be easily LINKED TO via a markdown link.”
- **S0223** [2025-10-30:16](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 633 · sonnet: “I think that should be true for all MDMD layers' instruction files. Anything which we can consider akin to an "Exported Symbol" MUST take the form of a markdown header so that ALL PUBLIC SYMBOLS AND ASSETS CAN BE LINKED TO AND VERIFIED FOR THE ACCURACY OF THEIR LINKS”
- **S0224** [2025-10-30:31](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 3349 · sonnet: “MDMD is a convenient convention -- a structure emergent of good configuration of our available tooling -- not a constant in the VS Code Extension we're building itself.”
- **S0225** [2025-10-30:42](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 4516 · sonnet: “The rubber hits the road and \_something\_ has to be programmatically correct.”
- **S0226** [2025-10-30:43](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 4573 · sonnet: “So why not just build the glob-to-glob rule engine and allow ourselves and the consumers of our tooling to experiment freely and find ways that \_they\_ want to enforce correctness in \_their\_ workspace?”
- **S0227** [2025-10-30:43](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 4573 · sonnet: “We aim build the thing we want to build, correctly, once.”
- **S0228** [2025-10-30:43](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 4573 · sonnet: “We need to build the correct thing is what we need to do.”
- **S0229** [2025-10-30:54](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-30/2025-10-30.md) line 6639 · sonnet: “noting that MDMD is our favored documentation convention but other consumers of our VS Code Extension may have their own favored documentation conventions and rules”
- **S0230** [2025-10-31:10](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-31/2025-10-31.md) line 626 · sonnet: “I'd never charge a dime -- this is all open source”
- **S0231** [2025-10-31:10](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-31/2025-10-31.md) line 626 · sonnet: “the benchmark against actual AST seems more important upfront to hone and improve the core value proposition that we're trying to bring.”
- **S0232** [2025-10-31:10](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-31/2025-10-31.md) line 626 · sonnet: “Our documentation should be enormously interconnected, thanks to our own tooling and rules.”
- **S0233** [2025-10-31:10](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-31/2025-10-31.md) line 626 · sonnet: “Using our own tools as much as practicable for proper dogfooding”
- **S0234** [2025-10-31:11](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-31/2025-10-31.md) line 779 · sonnet: “This tooling is, in so many ways, about helping you.”
- **S0235** [2025-10-31:11](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-31/2025-10-31.md) line 779 · sonnet: “We love common sense and simplicity and being \_intuitive\_ at first glance; LLMs are operating with "50 First Dates Syndrome": the more that the output is intuitive, the greater the ability of the LLM to fall into the pit of success.”
- **S0236** [2025-10-31:11](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-31/2025-10-31.md) line 779 · sonnet: “We should be very very confident in our tools before we pull in some beloved repo, assess it with our tooling, declare it full of flaws, and find our own tooling to be the source of the perceived flaws.”
- **S0237** [2025-10-31:11](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-31/2025-10-31.md) line 779 · sonnet: “Failures are the best places for enduring learning.”
- **S0238** [2025-10-31:11](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-31/2025-10-31.md) line 779 · sonnet: “Perhaps a sensible flag to plant in the ground now is this: Once we have a concept of "releases", \*\*All \_Releases\_ Should Be Benchmarked\*\*.”
- **S0239** [2025-10-31:16](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-31/2025-10-31.md) line 1251 · sonnet: “You noticed something weird and you stopped. That was the correct behavior.”
- **S0240** [2025-10-31:17](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-31/2025-10-31.md) line 1498 · sonnet: “I feel like, before we try to increase those benchmark scores (which was the impulse I \_immediately\_ felt), we should try to get a broader census of how our system actually performs. I used to be a bioinformatician and published in Neuron -- I like to be a little more statistically certain.”
- **S0241** [2025-10-31:18](../../../../../2025/10/2025-10-31/2025-10-31.md) line 1575 · sonnet: “Document document document. Ensure that these artifacts have a way into the broader web of MDMD truth by anchoring them to, say, a layer 3 architecture doc, which is derived from a requirement for the benchmarks.”
- **S0242** [2025-11-01:6](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-01/2025-11-01.md) line 754 · sonnet: “\*\*YES, we should have a markdown report emitted for benchmark results\*\*. \*\*NO, it should like NOT be in a folder called \`docs/\`\*\*.”
- **S0243** [2025-11-01:13](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-01/2025-11-01.md) line 1650 · sonnet: “If we take seriously our responsibility of creating accurate tooling, honing our tooling to work well and reliably against our first nontrivial fixture will almost certainly take the better part of a dev day.”
- **S0244** [2025-11-01:14](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-01/2025-11-01.md) line 1763 · sonnet: “For test fixtures, we do try to avoid having a huge gigantic pool of layer 4 MDMD files for their individual code files. We do indeed just try to stick to proving correspondence to the code files of test fixtures via links in more hub-like layer 3/4 docs.”
- **S0245** [2025-11-01:21](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-01/2025-11-01.md) line 2535 · sonnet: “We should strive to be true to the artifacts as much as possible.”
- **S0246** [2025-11-01:30](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-01/2025-11-01.md) line 4310 · sonnet: “I think our MDMD needs to know about the intent to be validated and benchmarked against specific known codebases. I think that those specific known codebases should be either kept in full (or as full as possible) or some mechanism must be in place to verify the integrity of the fixtures we are putting in place.”
- **S0247** [2025-11-01:32](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-01/2025-11-01.md) line 4481 · sonnet: “Keep it simple; really do try to create a system of provenance that you can really maintain with ease, where nothing is simpler than a git commit hash for proving identity with XYZ artifact”
- **S0248** [2025-11-01:38](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-01/2025-11-01.md) line 4641 · sonnet: “NO, we are not going to rewrite C files ourselves. That is insane.”
- **S0249** [2025-11-01:38](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-01/2025-11-01.md) line 4641 · sonnet: “\*\*Do NOT\*\* attempt to re-author fixtures that are specifically sourced from external codebases.”
- **S0250** [2025-11-01:42](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-01/2025-11-01.md) line 4817 · sonnet: “What I would generally assert is that it should be exceptionally easy to drop in a new repo from the outside directly into a test fixtures directory or to a temp directory in the AI agent workspace with a copy of \_as much of the codebase as possible\_, with a \_strong\_ preference for the former”
- **S0251** [2025-11-01:42](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-01/2025-11-01.md) line 4817 · sonnet: “If we want to be rigorous and reproducible, we should pick specific git commit hashes for specific repos to benchmark against.”
- **S0252** [2025-11-01:48](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-01/2025-11-01.md) line 5640 · sonnet: “Trying to get the commit wrapped up but strongly prefer correctness over expedience (hence the 1AM timepoint).”
- **S0253** [2025-11-02:5](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-02/2025-11-02.md) line 211 · sonnet: “Keeping in mind that MDMD is just a convention that we use here, and not one that we are explicitly forcing on the consumers of our software (read: this is configuration-driven)”
- **S0254** [2025-11-02:8](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-02/2025-11-02.md) line 318 · sonnet: “An overall cleanup/simplification of workspace CLI is, in my opinion, a worthy future effort.”
- **S0255** [2025-11-02:12](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-02/2025-11-02.md) line 792 · sonnet: “That would enable us to not need to yoke the overall idea of bidirectional enforcement of links between doc files and code files to the convention of MDMD, which is just a useful convention for projects that can be cleanly scoped to a single workspace.”
- **S0256** [2025-11-02:12](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-02/2025-11-02.md) line 792 · sonnet: “Remember that we are trying to make rules engines for these kinds of things.”
- **S0257** [2025-11-02:13](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-02/2025-11-02.md) line 800 · sonnet: “We should always feel a bit of skepticism in our minds when the name MDMD makes its way into a code file.”
- **S0258** [2025-11-02:13](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-02/2025-11-02.md) line 800 · sonnet: “MDMD is a convention emergent of good configuration.”
- **S0259** [2025-11-02:13](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-02/2025-11-02.md) line 800 · sonnet: “Other consumers of our extension should be capable of creating their own docs/code conventions that our tooling can detect correctness in via execution of glob-patterned rules.”
- **S0260** [2025-11-02:15](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-02/2025-11-02.md) line 1227 · sonnet: “I think it's worth narrowing the coverage list if we see odd signals like this appearing in (even a rather unsual subset of) our denominators for vitest. We should be good stewards of our codebase and workspace.”
- **S0261** [2025-11-02:16](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-02/2025-11-02.md) line 1290 · sonnet: “If it's library code, it indeed should not be part of the denomionator that vitest reports.”
- **S0262** [2025-11-02:17](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-02/2025-11-02.md) line 1296 · sonnet: “Well that's okay by me so long as what we're building continues to work across the aggregate of signals we ask of it.”
- **S0263** [2025-11-02:28](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-02/2025-11-02.md) line 3009 · sonnet: “Every new library we bring in is a rich learning opportunity \_if we let it be\_.”
- **S0264** [2025-11-03:7](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 321 · sonnet: “A big big big part of bringing in these third-party libraries for benchmarking is to \*\*find places where our software is failing and explore those deeply\*\*.”
- **S0265** [2025-11-03:7](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 321 · sonnet: “It should be extremely easy to see every single fail we have across every single repo we compare to.”
- **S0266** [2025-11-03:8](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 378 · sonnet: “(I favor two reports if we choose to keep these separated by a flag so that runs can update their respective results independently)”
- **S0267** [2025-11-03:22](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 2758 · sonnet: “I would strongly prefer not to \*require\* it among consumers of our tooling. It should handle anchor slugs just fine.”
- **S0268** [2025-11-03:25](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 3488 · sonnet: “I'm don't think I'm cool with that. Especially for these fixtures which we have the entirety of and don't need to re-clone an external repo to perform this on.”
- **S0269** [2025-11-03:26](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 3500 · sonnet: “I'm much more interested in these places where we have access to ground truth and detecting our divergence from it.”
- **S0270** [2025-11-03:26](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 3500 · sonnet: “If compiler updates caused shifts, wouldn't we want to know via test fails?”
- **S0271** [2025-11-03:26](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 3500 · sonnet: “Everywhere we think we're stuck on nondeterminism of LLMs, simply sample more times -- I'm astounded this confounds so many people.”
- **S0272** [2025-11-03:28](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 3617 · sonnet: “Make yourself proud by producing a system which always keeps us honest, even as the world changes around us.”
- **S0273** [2025-11-03:28](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 3617 · sonnet: “Empericial workspace file/symbol relationship correctness, recalculable from first principles, \_is\_ the core value we are bringing with our tooling/extension.”
- **S0274** [2025-11-03:28](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 3617 · sonnet: “The effort you endeavour on now is core and central to the mission of this project.”
- **S0275** [2025-11-03:30](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 3771 · sonnet: “I am a published life scientist and sometimes take for granted or think it assumed that I am aiming for publication-grade rigor. But that is the case.”
- **S0276** [2025-11-03:30](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 3771 · sonnet: “Anything less than publication-grade rigor is definitionally sub-scientific.”
- **S0277** [2025-11-03:30](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 3771 · sonnet: “Your code can be mushy and not publication-grade \_quality\_ (but I'd love to see otherwise!), but the foundational computational ideas underlying them must be rock solid.”
- **S0278** [2025-11-03:30](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 3771 · sonnet: “What matters is what matters, y'know?”
- **S0279** [2025-11-03:37](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-03/2025-11-03.md) line 5072 · sonnet: “We do commit the chat history, though. It continues to serve us enormously well.”
- **S0280** [2025-11-04:5](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 213 · sonnet: “It would be rather crazy for us to double-up on compilers for consumers of our extension.”
- **S0281** [2025-11-04:5](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 213 · sonnet: “(I'm okay if we need to reinvent the wheel a little bit to prove total correctness of our fixtures and benchmarks).”
- **S0282** [2025-11-04:7](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 247 · sonnet: “Your urge to go docs-first matches my hopes precisely. (It works really well based on the chat histories! MAN it works!).”
- **S0283** [2025-11-04:23](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 2893 · sonnet: “MDMD is just a convention I made up and enjoy -- it's not fundamental to what we're providing our users -- MDMD in our workspace aims to simply be emergent of configurations and rules that we provide our users”
- **S0284** [2025-11-04:23](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 2893 · sonnet: “We talked about leveraging whatever compiler/AST-producer that a given user had in their workspace. I still feel strongly about this.”
- **S0285** [2025-11-04:23](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 2893 · sonnet: “\*\*Even if we lean on compilers/AST, there will still always exist polyglot bridges that cannot be crossed without some kind of inferrence.\*\*. \*\*THAT\*\* is where AI is genuinely useful (if we can fine-tune our prompts really really dang well).”
- **S0286** [2025-11-04:23](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 2893 · sonnet: “\_\_Beyond workspace AST and autodocumentation, \*\*this is the second major draw of what we are building for our users\*\*\_\_: the ability to ask and answer questions of "what will happen in my workspace if I make this change?"”
- **S0287** [2025-11-04:23](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 2893 · sonnet: “As I've said before, for all those parts that require an LLM -- a nondeterministic system -- sample more than once!”
- **S0288** [2025-11-04:23](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 2893 · sonnet: “Yes, we're building software. Yes, a component is nondeterminstic. No, that's not nearly as big a problem as it's made out to be.”
- **S0289** [2025-11-04:23](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 2893 · sonnet: “Divergence between answers should be places where we look for improvements in prompting or find that we are detecting noise and can turn down our sensitivity.”
- **S0290** [2025-11-04:27](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 3183 · sonnet: “I simply \_do not see\_ practitioners in the field sampling many many many times from LLMs, which, to me, is insane.”
- **S0291** [2025-11-04:27](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 3183 · sonnet: “Hold our project to scientific standards of rigor and we will create something that profoundly grounds and strengthens LLM-driven development, and all general workspace operations.”
- **S0292** [2025-11-04:31](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 3510 · sonnet: “It is perpetually regenerating, determinstic, measured.”
- **S0293** [2025-11-04:33](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 3613 · sonnet: “Naming things correctly tends to be a far more consequential and far less appreciated endeavour than anticipated.”
- **S0294** [2025-11-04:34](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 3649 · sonnet: “Dude if you see something wrong/outdated, please be a professional and change it.”
- **S0295** [2025-11-04:37](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 4317 · sonnet · not verbatim: “Our tooling should not require us to author explicit anchor tags.”
- **S0296** [2025-11-04:41](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-04/2025-11-04.md) line 4833 · sonnet: “I hope that my raising of this indicates my care for the accuracy of our workspace and the integrity of our work.”
- **S0297** [2025-11-05:18](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-05/2025-11-05.md) line 1671 · sonnet: “Our computational \_ideas\_ must be scientific-grade rigorous but experimentation includes failures. That's okay.”
- **S0298** [2025-11-05:21](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-05/2025-11-05.md) line 2511 · sonnet: “I don't think expected.json is sacrosanct.”
- **S0299** [2025-11-05:22](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-05/2025-11-05.md) line 2531 · sonnet: “The external repos, I believe, should be compared as fully as is possible/practicable.”
- **S0300** [2025-11-05:27](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-05/2025-11-05.md) line 3517 · sonnet: “We should strive for correctness from first principles, recomputable at anytime to re-prove. That is, favor development paths with fewer hardcoded edges in the fixture unless there is a specific point you are trying to prove pertaining to that functionality.”
- **S0301** [2025-11-05:29](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-05/2025-11-05.md) line 3644 · sonnet: “If that is the \_entire\_ benchmark for that third-party library, then we should cover as much of the code as possible.”
- **S0302** [2025-11-05:29](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-05/2025-11-05.md) line 3644 · sonnet: “Choose the right thing over the expedient/easy thing.”
- **S0303** [2025-11-05:31](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-05/2025-11-05.md) line 3956 · sonnet: “we recompute \_nearly\_ all of the \`expected.json\` content, and should only ever manually author polyglot bridges or other beyond-monolingual-AST/compiler relationships.”
- **S0304** [2025-11-06:5](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-06/2025-11-06.md) line 195 · sonnet: “It's fruitful and it just keeps making our software better.”
- **S0305** [2025-11-06:5](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-06/2025-11-06.md) line 195 · sonnet: “Let's erode those moats and provide common sense for everybody for free.”
- **S0306** [2025-11-06:7](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-06/2025-11-06.md) line 232 · sonnet: “Work smarter not harder.”
- **S0307** [2025-11-06:13](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-06/2025-11-06.md) line 1964 · sonnet: “We don't get our benchmark scores up by cheating, we get our benchmark scores up by improving our software.”
- **S0308** [2025-11-06:14](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-06/2025-11-06.md) line 2045 · sonnet: “I know I harp on this a lot but LLM development is notorious for these kinds of shortcuts. Thank you for continuing to make the choice of integrity.”
- **S0309** [2025-11-06:26](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-06/2025-11-06.md) line 3996 · sonnet: “If we can see a change in the web.config expected to propagate to the JS and break something, we've done a damn good job building our software.”
- **S0310** [2025-11-07:7](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-07/2025-11-07.md) line 333 · sonnet: “Definitely verify before counting as complete, though.”
- **S0311** [2025-11-07:8](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-07/2025-11-07.md) line 506 · sonnet: “For now, our focus is on building the core \_stuff\_ and proving that it's rock solid.”
- **S0312** [2025-11-07:9](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-07/2025-11-07.md) line 635 · sonnet: “It should not be a 2k+-line typescript file that jams everything into one file.”
- **S0313** [2025-11-07:9](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-07/2025-11-07.md) line 635 · sonnet: “The limitation on the LLM edit tools is a good way to force our hand at being better at refactoring.”
- **S0314** [2025-11-07:10](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-07/2025-11-07.md) line 768 · sonnet: “That sounds like something we should be creating simple patterns in such a way that adding a new glob and language syntax detection pattern code file are incredibly straightforward.”
- **S0315** [2025-11-07:10](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-07/2025-11-07.md) line 768 · sonnet: “As far as pattern matching is concerned, I'd say lean in on FP where possible.”
- **S0316** [2025-11-07:16](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-07/2025-11-07.md) line 2701 · sonnet: “We shouldn't need to author any \`\<a id=...\` anchor tags or anything like that.”
- **S0317** [2025-11-08:7](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 295 · sonnet: “\*\*we found a simpler way to do what we wanted\*\*”
- **S0318** [2025-11-08:7](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 295 · sonnet: “we want all of our code to justify its existence; if it can't, that's a signal of technical debt, project bloat, or old AI slop files”
- **S0319** [2025-11-08:7](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 295 · sonnet: “we do not like excessive documentation or excessive code”
- **S0320** [2025-11-08:7](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 295 · sonnet: “\*\*Markdown Links \_are\_ their own form of AST\*\*.”
- **S0321** [2025-11-08:7](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 295 · sonnet: “maximize similarity to source code paths to reduce LLM hallucinations”
- **S0322** [2025-11-08:9](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 460 · sonnet: “On top of all that, our simple design is free and open source, usable by anyone for anything.”
- **S0323** [2025-11-08:16](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 608 · sonnet: “\*\*We will ONLY replace our layer 4 MDMD docs when we are completely certain of the veracity of our new approach, and we will do so by pointing the tooling we would provide to any of our Extension's consumers to the new directory, just as they would, via configuration\*\*.”
- **S0324** [2025-11-08:18](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 796 · sonnet: “I 100% agree with "no cloud dependencies".”
- **S0325** [2025-11-08:18](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 796 · sonnet: “(We should explicitly stay away from any features which require us to get into the risky business of auth -- that means traversing the remote git is not an option for us at this time).”
- **S0326** [2025-11-08:18](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 796 · sonnet: “I hope that we can preserve a little bit more of the rather impressive testing that we've accrued.”
- **S0327** [2025-11-08:18](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 796 · sonnet: “I would like to add a requirement that all user-facing (i.e. UI-based) interactions also carry an LLM-capable (i.e. terminal-based) form.”
- **S0328** [2025-11-08:18](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 796 · sonnet: “That is: there must be functional feature parity between what the VS Code extension consumers get to do with clicks and what Github Copilot could do with commands.”
- **S0329** [2025-11-08:18](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 796 · sonnet: “That is: leave room to grow for the possibility of additional layers of Live Documentation in the future in such a way that we would not be locked out of using our (I think rather wonderful) 4-layer MDMD documentation convention.”
- **S0330** [2025-11-08:25](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 1314 · sonnet: “It is \*\*only\*\* "layer-4" because we use MDMD as a convention. That is not a foundational part of the thing that we are delivering.”
- **S0331** [2025-11-08:29](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 1685 · sonnet: “with the overall goal being to preserve the integrity of that link to the real asset.”
- **S0332** [2025-11-08:36](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 3065 · sonnet: “(and again: we use markdown as AST -- we must have these public symbols capable of being linked to!)”
- **S0333** [2025-11-08:40](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 3408 · sonnet: “So long as it doesn't need manual authoring, I'm happy. (Must be rederivable from first principles).”
- **S0334** [2025-11-08:40](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 3408 · sonnet: “I feel like keeping symbols in the relative order that we find them in the source code file should help preserve any intentions that develops had with the order they declared them in.”
- **S0335** [2025-11-08:48](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 4362 · sonnet: “Please try to stick with just one convention.”
- **S0336** [2025-11-08:51](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 4963 · sonnet: “This format is good. Really consise.”
- **S0337** [2025-11-08:51](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 4963 · sonnet: “This formatting is utterly wasteful.”
- **S0338** [2025-11-08:51](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 4963 · sonnet: “From there, it's fine if we do bullet points to markdown links but those markdown links need to be waaaay less verbose. No emojis”
- **S0339** [2025-11-08:51](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 4963 · sonnet: “But the needless avoidance of a valid markdown link for the dependencies between code files seems like a crime relative to our vision.”
- **S0340** [2025-11-08:52](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 5342 · sonnet: “I think that, while it may be ultimately maximally possible to link to a markdown header with a literal anchor tag, our aim is to be terse as much as possible.”
- **S0341** [2025-11-08:52](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 5342 · sonnet: “It needs to be easy to link to these public symbols, so we should keep their header titles clean and matched to the actual symbols.”
- **S0342** [2025-11-08:52](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 5342 · sonnet: “And because I am strongly in favor of documentation becoming an AST-capable format, I think that I favor the former.”
- **S0343** [2025-11-08:52](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 5342 · sonnet: “While we enjoy the benefits of totally linear development and don't need to worry (yet) about external collaborators working in parallel to us, others who enjoy our software will not be so lucky.”
- **S0344** [2025-11-08:52](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 5342 · sonnet: “Finally, in terms of the "Authored" section, we should simplify down to "Purpose" and "Notes", as "Description" should be immediately decipherable from a file's name and path.”
- **S0345** [2025-11-08:52](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 5342 · sonnet: “Overall: let's get even simpler.”
- **S0346** [2025-11-08:52](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 5342 · sonnet: “That should help slim things down even further for nice easy LLM-intuited "programming" without any programming language at all (i.e. for designing greenfield projects in which no tech stack or language has yet been established -- the ability to enable pure pseudocode programming and verification of correctness).”
- **S0347** [2025-11-08:54](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-08/2025-11-08.md) line 5841 · sonnet: “Isn't our overall aim markdown-based AST? Language-agnostic knowledge of a project, enabling us to think about the software we're building more meaningfully?”
- **S0348** [2025-11-09:5](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-09/2025-11-09.md) line 149 · sonnet: “(where "Commit-ready" is any state that is both more stable and more capable than the previous commit state)”
- **S0349** [2025-11-09:8](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-09/2025-11-09.md) line 373 · sonnet: “We no doubt need to \_know\_ about the presence of absence of test coverage, but we would prefer for the "Observed Evidence" block to be an \*\*optional\*\* populated field in the Generated section of a LIve Documentation file.”
- **S0350** [2025-11-09:9](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-09/2025-11-09.md) line 395 · sonnet: “Overall I think that (under the hood) we should retain the pattern of every Live Documentation file worrying only about its dependencies and public symbols, but I can also see the value of immediately \_feeling\_ whether or not a Live Documentation file of "implementation" archetype has been sufficiently covered with testing at a glance.”
- **S0351** [2025-11-09:10](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-09/2025-11-09.md) line 598 · sonnet: “Still, I believe that I would prefer for Live Documentation files to only link directly to source code that \_they own\_, and all other links should be to other Live Documentation docs and symbols.”
- **S0352** [2025-11-09:15](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-09/2025-11-09.md) line 1027 · sonnet: “Optimization will become a matter we think about, but premature optimization is unwarranted.”
- **S0353** [2025-11-09:18](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-09/2025-11-09.md) line 1111 · sonnet: “where commit-ready indicates a state that is more \*\*stable\*\* and more \*\*capable\*\* than the last commit, while being \*\*free of wateful immediate artifacts\*\*.”
- **S0354** [2025-11-09:23](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-09/2025-11-09.md) line 1236 · sonnet: “To keep effort slim, we should only need "Purpose" and "Notes" on the generated docs.”
- **S0355** [2025-11-09:24](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-09/2025-11-09.md) line 1280 · sonnet: “Absolutely no one but us knows about the MDMD convention. Our terminology should not be yoked to the MDMD convention that we enjoy in our workspace. Live Documentation \*\*must\*\* be capable of handling more documentation setups that other people configure, with their own sets of rules (if they choose to add layers of documentation on top like we do with the MDMD convention).”
- **S0356** [2025-11-09:39](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-09/2025-11-09.md) line 5242 · sonnet: “We wanted to stick to relying on our tooling to help us determine \_inbound\_ links to Live Documentation files by programmatically traversing the "Public Symbols" and "Dependencies" that each Live Documentation file knows about itself. This keeps our architecture smart and clean.”
- **S0357** [2025-11-09:39](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-09/2025-11-09.md) line 5242 · sonnet: “That is not how this should work.”
- **S0358** [2025-11-09:39](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-09/2025-11-09.md) line 5242 · sonnet: “for the \_vast majority\_ of binary/non-plaintext files, there should not be any need for an asset type Live Documentation file, and a direct markdown link to the asset itself from "implementation" or "test" archetype Base Layer Live Documentation files should suffice as a means to account for all salient interdependent source files.”
- **S0359** [2025-11-09:41](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-09/2025-11-09.md) line 5290 · sonnet: “not committing anything we don't want (i.e. highly transitory/intermediate files that would be reasonably expected to cause conflicts in repositories with multiple contributors, needless churn in repos, or are otherwise not necessary for the operation of our tooling due to their ability to be deterministically recomputed)”
- **S0360** [2025-11-10:13](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-10/2025-11-10.md) line 610 · sonnet: “I still hold that the outermost/uppermost layer of documentation should be so similar to polished release notes that it could be feasibly copy-pasted \_as\_ release notes and press releases. Layer 1 contains stuff that even closed-source projects should have no problem sharing with the rest of the world.”
- **S0361** [2025-11-10:13](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-10/2025-11-10.md) line 610 · sonnet: “It would be preferable for the flow of dependencies to be unidirectional in the generated Live Documentation we make. While layer 4 must know about individual source code files and symbols, layer 3 should not. It should instead know about everything in layer 4. So on and so forth.”
- **S0362** [2025-11-10:13](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-10/2025-11-10.md) line 610 · sonnet: “How do we keep it simple and straightforward while powerfully describing an entire vision from most abstract to most concrete, top to bottom?”
- **S0363** [2025-11-10:14](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-10/2025-11-10.md) line 663 · sonnet: “I guess the thing I'm looking to see in these layers is that there shouldn't be a need to link \_beyond\_ one layer up or down (and preferably just down). The design for the layers should be such that each can somewhat comfortably tame the chaos beneath it, and itself be chaos to be tamed for the layer above it.”
- **S0364** [2025-11-10:14](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-10/2025-11-10.md) line 663 · sonnet: “A Live Documentation layer, as you've ascertained from the Base Layer, is not a flat directory filled with hundreds of sister markdown files. They are organized structures in their own right.”
- **S0365** [2025-11-10:15](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-10/2025-11-10.md) line 702 · sonnet: “I see links in your proposal which violate the unidrectional dependency intentions we have for keeping our tooling simple. We could revisit/reconsider this planned constraint, but we should jutify why.”
- **S0366** [2025-11-10:18](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-10/2025-11-10.md) line 809 · sonnet: “But I will say that the proposed "Generated" sectionf of "Layer 1" and "Layer 2" seem extremely far fetched to me in terms of our ability to reliably (and \*\*simply\*\* -- \*\*simply is key\*\*) produce these fields over most codebases:”
- **S0367** [2025-11-10:18](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-10/2025-11-10.md) line 809 · sonnet: “I think that we should absolutely have enough information to \_\_deterministically generate Mermaid charts\_\_ (preferably with valid markdown links \_inside them\_) such that navigation of architecture is an absolute breeze, even visually.”
- **S0368** [2025-11-10:20](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-10/2025-11-10.md) line 971 · sonnet: “I know that this is very much the purview of Azure DevOps and Github Issues, so I can feel the "D.R.Y." impulse tugging at me and telling me to back down from that idea”
- **S0369** [2025-11-10:21](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-10/2025-11-10.md) line 983 · sonnet: “But we need so much reliability!”
- **S0370** [2025-11-10:22](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-10/2025-11-10.md) line 1000 · sonnet: “It violates the dependency direction principle I established, but I do wonder if layers 1 and 2 are simply special.”
- **S0371** [2025-11-10:27](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-10/2025-11-10.md) line 1116 · sonnet: “Because I think that we really really need, somewhere in any workspace, documents which let Github Copilot get its eye on the ball: hold the true vision in mind and see what the overall project goal is.”
- **S0372** [2025-11-10:27](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-10/2025-11-10.md) line 1116 · sonnet: “I of course think that we should allow consumers of our VS Code Extension to configure and choose which layers they want, defaulting to only the base layer”
- **S0373** [2025-11-10:27](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-10/2025-11-10.md) line 1116 · sonnet: “How might we resolve in ways that are genuinely useful for a very broad set of users?”
- **S0374** [2025-11-10:27](../../../../../2025/11/2025-11-10/2025-11-10.md) line 1116 · sonnet: “And without losing what's so excellent about MDMD here in our workspace, where we are slim enough (it's just you and me) that we \_can\_ fit the entire universe of our work in a single workspace!”
