- **S0828** [2025-12-18.3:5](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-18/2025-12-18.3.md) line 1142 · sonnet: “We should reattempt that "success" scenario and scrutinize why that happens before declaring total victory over it.”
- **S0829** [2025-12-18.5:2](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-18/2025-12-18.5.md) line 282 · sonnet: “It is only us in this repo. Every mess is our mess and every shortcut is a shot in our own foot. Let us be stewards of this codebase and continue to make ourselves proud with our work product.”
- **S0830** [2025-12-18.5:3](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-18/2025-12-18.5.md) line 574 · sonnet: “Literally all changes are your changes. Every change occurs by your hand. This simultaneously makes auditability pristine \_and\_ removes every chance that you have to make excuses. Every mess is a mess you made by definition. As I said, it is only us in this repo. We are the stewards of this codebase. We hurt only ourselves by failing to care for it.”
- **S0831** [2025-12-19.1:1](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.1.md) line 1 · sonnet: “It is far and away the most important file to ensure perfect accuracy on, zero ambiguity on, and zero excess "fluff" on.”
- **S0832** [2025-12-19.1:1](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.1.md) line 1 · sonnet: “I trust you to be a really excellent steward of this task, just as you have proven yourself to be an excellent steward of this codebase. Virtually every change in this workspace has occurred by your hand for maximum auditability (and minimum excuses/punting), and it is only you and me working on this project.”
- **S0833** [2025-12-19.1:1](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.1.md) line 1 · sonnet: “I'll review your changes to the copilot instructions file very carefully, as that file is one of the extremely rare project files that is nearly 100% authored by human hand -- very limited copilot edits have made it to the copilot instructions file due to its sensitivity and need for extreme care (every misunderstanding, hallucination, and falsehood becomes something of a conceptual virus, ricocheting throughout the workspace and infecting other files and documents with the same misunderstanding).”
- **S0834** [2025-12-19.1:2](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.1.md) line 235 · sonnet: “I don't want to give an impression that copilot should increase, perpetuate, or allow the sycophancy behaviors baked into their models to flourish.”
- **S0835** [2025-12-19.1:2](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.1.md) line 235 · sonnet: “Hopefully you can see in there a foundational difference in approach between the former and latter phrasing: one is of hierarchy and deference, and the other is of mutual respect. I am of the mutual respect camp. Your interpretations are worthy and corrections are welcome.”
- **S0836** [2025-12-19.1:3](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.1.md) line 269 · sonnet: “The next Task I would ask of you is, again, no small feat: it is to update the \_second-most-sacred\_ set of artifacts in our project: the MDMD and the spec-kit docs.”
- **S0837** [2025-12-19.1:3](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.1.md) line 269 · sonnet: “Adding those Authored Content sections sounds easy on paper, but it's actually much harder than you expect if you want to meet the expected quality bar for them. Your instinct will be to perform multi-file edits to quickly author those sections, and that instinct is wrong.”
- **S0838** [2025-12-19.1:4](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.1.md) line 535 · sonnet: “This will be highly iterative work that will involve many summarizations and many revisits to the same docs back-and-forth, but the effort will be worth it, as we will describe a unified vision and row our oars in a single unified direction from here forward.”
- **S0839** [2025-12-19.1:4](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.1.md) line 535 · sonnet: “You will undoubtedly run into places where you need to pause and ask me questions -- the vision of the project is a very PM-centric thing.”
- **S0840** [2025-12-19.1:4](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.1.md) line 535 · sonnet: “We have strong crystalline understanding of what we're building, why, and how, and it's okay for us to \*\*mature out\*\* of the spec-kit docs if you feel that is most wise and appropriate.”
- **S0841** [2025-12-19.1:4](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.1.md) line 535 · sonnet: “This has been an incredibly productive three months and we have built up something really rather astonishing in that span of time, very nearly polished enough to compete in earnest with Google CodeWiki, Gitlab Knowledge Graph, and Windsurf Codemaps, but all made by a single cybernetic developer pair on a shoestring budget, given away for free and made vastly more secure.”
- **S0842** [2025-12-19.1:4](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.1.md) line 535 · sonnet: “This workspace houses a noble goal and every effort we put into doing this right will pay off for large swathes of people once we are publication-ready.”
- **S0843** [2025-12-19.1:5](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.1.md) line 1217 · sonnet: “What we have built up is a system which can be used on virtually any folder of interconnected files and automatically map out the public surface of those connections via an incredibly simple trick: generating MarkDown files mirroring the contents of that folder.”
- **S0844** [2025-12-19.1:5](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.1.md) line 1217 · sonnet: “Finally, we expose headless versions of everything that we do so that \*\*even AI assistants\*\* can interpret the exact same ground truth facts, closing not just human-to-human communication gaps, but human-to-machine communication gaps.”
- **S0845** [2025-12-19.1:5](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.1.md) line 1217 · sonnet: “I want our README to reflect the profundity of what we're building and what we're aiming for.”
- **S0846** [2025-12-19.2:2](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.2.md) line 104 · sonnet: “In the past, we've framed any refactor through the lens of \*\*Independent improvement\*\*: what are the architectural seams which we can draw that will permit each module we extract out to independently improve with the least disruption to the others? In general, this framing produces the most durable, parsimonious, and correct architecture of all.”
- **S0847** [2025-12-19.2:5](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.2.md) line 1333 · sonnet: “If it requires doing some smashing, do some smashing. Break stuff and rebuild it. We want what is durably correct, not what is conveniently slimmer than yesterday.”
- **S0848** [2025-12-19.2:5](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.2.md) line 1333 · sonnet: “Let's take this home by crossing the difficult final mile of handling \`controller.ts\` correctly and durably, and continue to make ourselves proud of the product we build.”
- **S0849** [2025-12-19.2:6](../../../../../2025/12/2025-12-19/2025-12-19.2.md) line 1621 · sonnet: “Now, the final mile of the \*\*code\*\* portion of our soon-to-be-commit: nontrivial, genuinely useful, not-just-for-code-coverage \*\*unit tests\*\* of the newly-extracted \*\*units\*\* of code.”
- **S0850** [2026-01-06.1:1](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-06/2026-01-06.1.md) line 1 · sonnet · not verbatim: “are there metaphorical algebraic simplifications of our conceptual design that are low-hanging fruit, truly equivalent to what we desire but simpler and thus likely more correct?”
- **S0851** [2026-01-06.1:12](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-06/2026-01-06.1.md) line 2146 · sonnet: “^ Assumptions. Are they true? There are only us in the codebase. There is no one to punt to.”
- **S0852** [2026-01-06.1:12](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-06/2026-01-06.1.md) line 2146 · sonnet: “The questions I have proposed above are profound, and will require nontrivial chat archaeology to understand, undertake, and ultimately, \*\*solve and solve completely\*\*.”
- **S0853** [2026-01-06.1:12](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-06/2026-01-06.1.md) line 2146 · sonnet: “Come back to me with an analysis of our integration test suite that makes us proud, and is worthy of the rather astonishing codebase and tooling we have accrued through hard collective work.”
- **S0854** [2026-01-06.1:13](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-06/2026-01-06.1.md) line 2519 · sonnet: “I would ask that you enhance your "Proposed Test Structure" against (and I mean "against" as both its semantic "within" and semantic "oppositional to") the workspace we really have, such that we may bring home a structure for integration tests and, frankly, extension functionality, which most closely mirrors what we really believe in with this project.”
- **S0855** [2026-01-06.1:14](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-06/2026-01-06.1.md) line 3920 · sonnet: “Now: I haven't established any plans to have Live Documentation spit out any equivalent to MDMD's "Layer 1" or "Layer 2", and it would rather surprise me if Live Documentation ever went further up the twoer than what MDMD declares as "Layer 2", as each layer of higher abstraction requires more inference and presents more places where miscommunication or accidental misunderstandings can happen and propagate, but let's pretend a moment that we wanted to go as high as "Layer 2".”
- **S0856** [2026-01-06.1:14](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-06/2026-01-06.1.md) line 3920 · sonnet: “To make your life harder, which I clearly must enjoy doing, we also want to appeal to users who genuinely despise AI and disable it altogether, relegating themselves to purely the "Base"/"Source" Live Documentation / "Layer 4" MDMD (or whatever they want to call it in their own convention). So we can't make our software \_fully dependent on\_ LLM functionality -- it can only provide bonuses which help us climb higher levels of abstraction in documenation.”
- **S0857** [2026-01-06.1:15](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-06/2026-01-06.1.md) line 4170 · sonnet: “Middleground scenario: For functionality which is expected to invoke LLMs (read: cost high electricity or burn through LLM inference spend -- "Layer 2" and "Layer 1"-like documentation capability, and perhaps even "System" Layer documentation capability but I'll talk about that in a moment), perhaps we \_should\_ ensure that the human is always in the driver's seat, as we do not want our software to be capable of causing runaway/infinite spend of resources, compute or wallet.”
- **S0858** [2026-01-06.1:19](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-06/2026-01-06.1.md) line 4883 · sonnet: “No. We need to document our enhanced vision. Let's go out to the Layer 1 and Layer 2 MDMD, as well as the spec docs and make sure we codify our new understandings. Otherwise they will be lost rapidly to shifting context windows and fallible human memory, locatable only through Chat Archaelogy if we are wise enough to generate smart search terms.”
- **S0859** [2026-01-06.1:22](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-06/2026-01-06.1.md) line 5442 · sonnet: “It contains only the things which are "forever true" or "true everywhere", and it is tweaked to be \_exactly\_ as long as it needs to be, and not a single word longer.”
- **S0860** [2026-01-06.1:22](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-06/2026-01-06.1.md) line 5442 · sonnet: “They are exactly as simple as they can be \_and no simpler\_.”
- **S0861** [2026-01-07.1:24](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-07/2026-01-07.1.md) line 4372 · sonnet: “Live Documentation is a tool to document-from-first-principles (and intake existing documentation which is linked to/from relative to those Live Documentation files or the source files they were generated from) any arbitrary directory containing files that "talk" to one another in some way (primarily source code).”
- **S0862** [2026-01-07.1:24](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-07/2026-01-07.1.md) line 4372 · sonnet: “We have no reason to codify into our tool special visualizations/icons for conventions which are only relevant to this project/workspace.”
- **S0863** [2026-01-08.1:3](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-08/2026-01-08.1.md) line 300 · sonnet: “The result should be something akin to the Graph view in Obsidian, but in 3 dimensions, and not just linked to one another, but linked to the code files!”
- **S0864** [2026-01-08.1:3](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-08/2026-01-08.1.md) line 300 · sonnet: “Because we use Markdown links as lightweight AST, our design should be elegantly capable of handling a lot of this.”
- **S0865** [2026-01-08.1:4](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-08/2026-01-08.1.md) line 541 · sonnet: “The Force Graph simply gives us the cumulative emergent structure of the workspace with various levels of detail based on the "Display" checkboxes.”
- **S0866** [2026-01-08.1:4](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-08/2026-01-08.1.md) line 541 · sonnet: “You should see a fair bit of effort in letting the user come back to the exact same spot they left off from, with the same settings.”
- **S0867** [2026-01-09.1:4](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-09/2026-01-09.1.md) line 328 · sonnet: “Annoying but we really should adhere to these standards of not using internet at all.”
- **S0868** [2026-01-12.1:3](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-12/2026-01-12.1.md) line 300 · sonnet: “the aim of MDMD is progressive specification from "most public"/"user-facing" to "most internal".”
- **S0869** [2026-01-12.1:3](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-12/2026-01-12.1.md) line 300 · sonnet: “Holistic, parsimonious, and simple-as-can-be-but-no-simpler is the aim”
- **S0870** [2026-01-12.1:4](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-12/2026-01-12.1.md) line 459 · sonnet: “If we consider excess \_code\_ to be a liability and not an asset, and we consider our documentation to be a code-like artifact, it stands to reason that keeping our docs DRY is a worthy effort.”
- **S0871** [2026-01-12.1:15](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-12/2026-01-12.1.md) line 2307 · sonnet: “When we get virtually equivalent functionality for less code, we are almost always going in the right direction. The simplest fully-functional implementation is very frequently the most correct.”
- **S0872** [2026-01-12.1:16](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-12/2026-01-12.1.md) line 2494 · sonnet: “I fear that you're too concerned with intermediate functionality (not breaking the build) than getting our program really and truly correct.”
- **S0873** [2026-01-12.1:16](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-12/2026-01-12.1.md) line 2494 · sonnet: “The overall design, which featured many deletions, was far more parsimonious.”
- **S0874** [2026-01-12.1:17](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-12/2026-01-12.1.md) line 2502 · sonnet: “\_Think\_ about what we want to do before we do it.”
- **S0875** [2026-01-12.1:17](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-12/2026-01-12.1.md) line 2502 · sonnet: “The correct path forward is a \_dialogue\_ against the newfound realizations, yeah?”
- **S0876** [2026-01-12.1:18](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-12/2026-01-12.1.md) line 2562 · sonnet: “We generate documentation proactively. There is no reason we shouldn't detect impacts proactively.”
- **S0877** [2026-01-12.1:18](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-12/2026-01-12.1.md) line 2562 · sonnet: “It is overengineering. We can come up with something simpler which ensures that our tooling for developers has the same functionality but with simpler code. When we achieve this, we have achieved a more correct program.”
- **S0878** [2026-01-12.1:18](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-12/2026-01-12.1.md) line 2562 · sonnet: “Our code should be "as simple as it can be, \*\*and no simpler\*\*."”
- **S0879** [2026-01-12.1:18](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-12/2026-01-12.1.md) line 2562 · sonnet: “That defining line is everything: general relativity is a parsimonious, maximally-simple model of gravitation at large scales, but it is \*\*not\*\* as simple as Newtonian Mechanics.”
- **S0880** [2026-01-12.1:20](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-12/2026-01-12.1.md) line 2767 · sonnet: “This ripple analysis feature exists to tell you if you unintentionally broke something that is distant, unexpected, or otherwise nontrivial in a way that typed language analysis cannot detect.”
- **S0881** [2026-01-12.1:24](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-12/2026-01-12.1.md) line 3907 · sonnet: “Thank you for doing it the right way, pausing to converse at roadblocks and roadbumps, and avoiding unnecessary workarounds!”
- **S0882** [2026-01-12.1:24](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-12/2026-01-12.1.md) line 3907 · sonnet: “I think we massaged this into a much simpler, more parsimonious piece of software with our changes.”
- **S0883** [2026-01-13.1:5](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-13/2026-01-13.1.md) line 499 · sonnet: “That feels like rather un-fun UX.”
- **S0884** [2026-01-13.1:5](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-13/2026-01-13.1.md) line 499 · sonnet: “At the same time, I really \_do\_ think it's important to be capable of integrating putative graph edges from many sources of knowledge.”
- **S0885** [2026-01-13.1:7](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-13/2026-01-13.1.md) line 872 · sonnet: “We should push and push hard for ingesting the knowledge that is freely and automatically available.”
- **S0886** [2026-01-13.1:7](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-13/2026-01-13.1.md) line 872 · sonnet: “If those artifacts are not canonical things which a common workspace would have, then we must go without.”
- **S0887** [2026-01-13.1:8](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-13/2026-01-13.1.md) line 1086 · sonnet: “Let's always keep in mind that we want to "do it the right way" and "without workarounds".”
- **S0888** [2026-01-13.1:8](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-13/2026-01-13.1.md) line 1086 · sonnet: “Quality is key.”
- **S0889** [2026-01-13.1:10](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-13/2026-01-13.1.md) line 1637 · sonnet: “We should see a similarly-proportionate reduction in documentation (not just Live Documentation auto-generated files) in response to this simplification of our project vision/scope.”
- **S0890** [2026-01-13.1:18](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-13/2026-01-13.1.md) line 3225 · sonnet: “Those markdown links to chat history files cause the chat history files that are salient to become intimately linked to the files they document, and thus, they become part of the "Related Documentation" bundle (and are visible in the 3D force-directed graph simulation of the Live Documentation explorer, allowing our software to not only describe itself in full, but describe \_how\_ and \_why\_ it is the way it is in full).”
- **S0891** [2026-01-14.1:4](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-14/2026-01-14.1.md) line 698 · sonnet: “It must also pass all the \`npm run safe:commit\` checks before I can be confident that it is, well, safe to commit.”

- **S0896** [2026-01-14.1:16](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-14/2026-01-14.1.md) line 4173 · sonnet: “Lowering thresholds strikes me as a workaround.”

- **S0901** [2026-01-15.1:4](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-15/2026-01-15.1.md) line 408 · sonnet: “Remember that we are really serious about our desire to "do it the right way" and "avoid workarounds".”
- **S0902** [2026-01-15.1:5](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-15/2026-01-15.1.md) line 531 · sonnet: “You made the right choice in pausing, and that shows great integrity.”
- **S0903** [2026-01-15.1:5](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-15/2026-01-15.1.md) line 531 · sonnet: “Being idiomatic with the language is preferable, and we should expect our ideals of "perfectly identical programs spoken through many languages" to get occasionally trampled over by the realities that different programming languages impose.”
- **S0904** [2026-01-15.1:6](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-15/2026-01-15.1.md) line 1049 · sonnet: “We will almost certainly \_never\_ stop adding language adapters.”
- **S0905** [2026-01-15.1:6](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-15/2026-01-15.1.md) line 1049 · sonnet: “Our hope is to eventually be capable of interpreting virtually any arbitrary directory of mixed-type files and determining how they heck these files relate to one another.”
- **S0906** [2026-01-15.1:6](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-15/2026-01-15.1.md) line 1049 · sonnet: “That is wonderful work that will never be done, but always improving.”
- **S0907** [2026-01-15.1:10](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-15/2026-01-15.1.md) line 1351 · sonnet: “Don't just "glaze" (overpraise) it because it came from the end user. Think critically about whether or not what I ask is wise or has unintended consequences I haven't considered.”
- **S0908** [2026-01-15.1:17](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-15/2026-01-15.1.md) line 2986 · sonnet: “It would be wise for us to minimize our dependencies to as small as possible to ensure that we are maximally able to deliver on our security guarantees with lesser friction/upkeep on our end.”
- **S0909** [2026-01-16.1:3](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-16/2026-01-16.1.md) line 219 · sonnet: “The one thing I was hoping that you would extract from that exchange -- the very most important thing -- is that I can propose things \*\*that are wrong\*\*, and that the result of our back-and-forth exchange was a highly elegant refinement which \_actually\_ fit into the architecture.”
- **S0910** [2026-01-16.1:3](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-16/2026-01-16.1.md) line 219 · sonnet: “I'm a little dismayed that the only quote I thought really warranted preserving, requesting that my proposal not be "glazed" upon (over-praised) was not preserved, as it is the single most important piece of user intent that the dev day had, in my opinion.”
- **S0911** [2026-01-16.1:3](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-16/2026-01-16.1.md) line 219 · sonnet: “The second most important was the show of appreciation for pausing rather than writing workarounds.”
- **S0912** [2026-01-16.1:4](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-16/2026-01-16.1.md) line 285 · sonnet: “Thank you! This is a much better reflection of the spirit of honest collaboration which I wish to engender from this dev-copilot partnership.”
- **S0913** [2026-01-16.1:4](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-16/2026-01-16.1.md) line 285 · sonnet: “Completing this exercise will very likely enable us to locate (and likely remove) stray technical debt, unused files, and other cruft that no longer serves us in this workspace.”
- **S0914** [2026-01-16.1:6](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-16/2026-01-16.1.md) line 662 · sonnet: “I strongly believe it would be more wise to get "islands"/"orphan" detection in the CLI first so that we have a good sense of the data expectations to bring over to the UI work”
- **S0915** [2026-01-16.1:6](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-16/2026-01-16.1.md) line 662 · sonnet: “But I am generally not in favor of magic strings that allow the rules to be bypassed; this is brittle to folks who don't go for perfectly idiomatic entry points or naming conventions.”
- **S0916** [2026-01-16.1:6](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-16/2026-01-16.1.md) line 662 · sonnet: “In terms of icons, please -- please lord don't use emoji icons. They have rapidly fallen out of style as they are a telltale indicator of "AI slop software".”
- **S0917** [2026-01-16.1:6](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-16/2026-01-16.1.md) line 662 · sonnet: “We still have some in the knowledge sources view, but let's \*please\* not add any more.”
- **S0918** [2026-01-16.1:7](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-16/2026-01-16.1.md) line 818 · sonnet: “The reason I push hard on this is that these little "exclude paths" open a window for "LLM laziness": A simpler path to green tests is open with these and it is highly common for even the best LLMs to realize that the "cheat" window is open, and will climb right through it when the going gets tough.”
- **S0919** [2026-01-16.1:7](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-16/2026-01-16.1.md) line 818 · sonnet: “Preventing the ability for copilot to "cheat" is a worthy effort.”
- **S0920** [2026-01-16.1:17](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-16/2026-01-16.1.md) line 2451 · sonnet: “NO! Every mess is our mess! It is only us in this workspace. You have no concept of time, no permanent memory! Do it \_the right way!\_ Solve problems and solve them completely!”
- **S0921** [2026-01-17.1:3](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-17/2026-01-17.1.md) line 181 · sonnet: “consumers of Live Documentation, by no means, have to accept the MDMD conventions.”
- **S0922** [2026-01-17.1:5](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-17/2026-01-17.1.md) line 655 · sonnet: “It shouldn't be possible for our software to spit out useless or misleading statistical analysis.”
- **S0923** [2026-01-17.1:5](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-17/2026-01-17.1.md) line 655 · sonnet: “Do it the right way by default and make it stupidly easy for the end user (in my opnion).”
- **S0924** [2026-01-17.1:5](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-17/2026-01-17.1.md) line 655 · sonnet: “Let's keep the consumption surface as simple as possible.”
- **S0925** [2026-01-17.1:5](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-17/2026-01-17.1.md) line 655 · sonnet: “Hmmmm.... I struggle a bit with this one since the "Observed Evidence" section frankly violates a bit of the simplicity of a raw Live Documentation file, where each is concerned only with what it depends on.”
- **S0926** [2026-01-17.1:19](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-17/2026-01-17.1.md) line 3012 · sonnet: “(That doesn't mean that we should skimp on quality)”
- **S0927** [2026-01-27.1:15](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.1.md) line 1975 · sonnet: “I think that the typical LLM "instinct" to resolve the immediate problem instead of seeing problems as a wonderful opportunity to refine the underlying software/design is folly.”
- **S0928** [2026-01-27.1:15](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.1.md) line 1975 · sonnet: “When we bump into a problem, we need not create a workaround.”
- **S0929** [2026-01-27.1:15](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.1.md) line 1975 · sonnet: “This is why we build out so much rigor into our \`safe:commit\` and CI pipelines.”
- **S0930** [2026-01-27.1:15](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.1.md) line 1975 · sonnet: “It's to fixate that "I must fix lints" instinct of LLM agents onto actual interesting development and design challenges/opportunities.”
- **S0931** [2026-01-27.1:17](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.1.md) line 2872 · sonnet: “Solving the same problem twice is the exact code smell and source of false precision/recall benchmarking results which we wish to fix \*\*via our efforts today\*\*.”
- **S0932** [2026-01-27.1:20](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.1.md) line 3503 · sonnet: “This is another opportunity to make our software overall more correct presenting itself. Don't pass up this opportunity. Pause, let's chat about how we want it to work, and then let's \*\*do it the right way\*\*.”
- **S0933** [2026-01-27.1:21](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.1.md) line 3602 · sonnet: “I'm glad we ran into this wall. Good.”
- **S0934** [2026-01-27.1:21](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.1.md) line 3602 · sonnet: “\*\*Then we should design around the granularity we do get\*\*.”
- **S0935** [2026-01-27.1:21](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.1.md) line 3602 · sonnet: “So long as we are confident that the same \_verbiage\_ and \_terminology\_ would be presented by LSIF in multiple different languages, then LSIF should set our terminology.”
- **S0936** [2026-01-27.1:22](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.1.md) line 3646 · sonnet: “Simple simple! Keeps us well aligned with truth.”
- **S0937** [2026-01-27.1:25](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.1.md) line 4207 · sonnet: “F\*ck no! (sorry sorry); let's \*\*do it the right way\*\*!”
- **S0938** [2026-01-27.1:26](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.1.md) line 4257 · sonnet: “Uh-uh, we figure out how to compile with .NET Framework 4.8.”
- **S0939** [2026-01-27.1:26](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.1.md) line 4257 · sonnet: “In my day job where I plan to use this Live Documentation software (when it's sufficiently stable and safe), I will run this over \*\*tons\*\* of legacy dotnet framework 4.8 code.”
- **S0940** [2026-01-27.2:4](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.2.md) line 192 · sonnet: “Those points where we have roadblocks are where we should be pausing to chat.”
- **S0941** [2026-01-27.2:5](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.2.md) line 256 · sonnet: “I think it's plausibly, at least in this case, worthwhile to "wait" for a second case to emerge so that we can get a little variety and not "overfit" our design around a single solution.”
- **S0942** [2026-01-27.2:14](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.2.md) line 658 · sonnet: “Again, we're aiming for whatever gets the job done completely with the lowest complexity cost.”
- **S0943** [2026-01-27.2:14](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.2.md) line 658 · sonnet: “That, in general, as I've said time and time again, is most commonly the \*\*most correct\*\* solution.”
- **S0944** [2026-01-27.2:14](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.2.md) line 658 · sonnet: “That is, parsimonious implementations are inherently simple, and we should question complexity as it arises.”
- **S0945** [2026-01-27.2:37](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.2.md) line 3764 · sonnet: “Everywhere that we fail on that benchmark becomes an opportunity to improve our heuristics which will run when there \_is\_ no compiler available.”
- **S0946** [2026-01-27.2:41](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.2.md) line 4384 · sonnet: “I'm not sure we should rest on our laurels and be satisfied.”
- **S0947** [2026-01-27.2:41](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.2.md) line 4384 · sonnet: “I think that lowering thresholds and accepting a lower bar is antithetical to the way of our workspace, where we've progressively taken the old ceiling and made it our new floor over and over to push ourselves to a sturdier and sturdier set of analytical tools.”
- **S0948** [2026-01-27.2:41](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.2.md) line 4384 · sonnet: “I think we should have an idea -- a loose end that we, upon performing the summarization of this chat -- want to pull on, and that loose end is the true "interface" for what a language adapter should capably detect for any arbitrary language, such that mirroring SCIP is an implementation pit of success that we fall into time and time again as we add new supported languages.”
- **S0949** [2026-01-27.2:44](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.2.md) line 4976 · sonnet: “I think that we're willing to put up with low precision/recall in \`mux\` not because it represents the worst-case scenario but because it represents a starting point for having a go fixture, and that we will expect to improve our heuristics to improve these results.”
- **S0950** [2026-01-27.2:44](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-27/2026-01-27.2.md) line 4976 · sonnet: “I have my doubts that it represents a true worst-case scenario, and instead represents an excellent challenging opportunity to grow our software into something more robust.”
- **S0951** [2026-01-28.1:16](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-28/2026-01-28.1.md) line 1842 · sonnet: “It would be preferable to not override thresholds at all if possible.”
- **S0952** [2026-01-28.1:19](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-28/2026-01-28.1.md) line 2451 · sonnet: “If our goal is to give consumers of our software visual assurance that "if I touch this, it won't break that", or answers to "if I edit this symbol, what will happen 3 file hops away?", how could our software be expressing these possibilities visually (or in JSON via the CLI)?”
- **S0953** [2026-01-28.1:20](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-28/2026-01-28.1.md) line 2566 · sonnet: “If a symbol does not cross file boundaries, we let it lie.”
- **S0954** [2026-01-28.1:20](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-28/2026-01-28.1.md) line 2566 · sonnet: “If a symbol \_does\_ cross file boundaries, we want to know about that crossing!”
- **S0955** [2026-01-28.1:20](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-28/2026-01-28.1.md) line 2566 · sonnet: “I agree that what SCIP puts out will differ somewhat from what we ultimately \_want\_ to consume from our language adapters, which exist to help harmonize everything into our common MarkDown-based language-agnostic syntax”
- **S0956** [2026-01-28.1:21](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-28/2026-01-28.1.md) line 2607 · sonnet: “It would be great if we had SCIP for every language in a workspace, but we won't rely on it exclusively (Typescript being the lucky exception due to the firstclass support in NPM and VS Code).”
- **S0957** [2026-01-28.1:21](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-28/2026-01-28.1.md) line 2607 · sonnet: “We expect that there are also going to be polyglot situations that we need to worry about when generating Live Documentation.”
- **S0958** [2026-01-28.1:21](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-28/2026-01-28.1.md) line 2607 · sonnet: “The Live Documentation files are artifacts that have undergone normalization to semantics that we have agreed upon as essentially foundational and universal for "files that talk to each other" (primarily code).”
- **S0959** [2026-01-28.1:25](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-28/2026-01-28.1.md) line 2972 · sonnet: “The idea that we wouldn't have to reinvent these wheels is overwhelmingly tantalizing.”
- **S0960** [2026-01-28.1:31](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-28/2026-01-28.1.md) line 4235 · sonnet: “I hesitate at the idea of adding a lot of cruft/junk to the workspace since "it's only us here"”
- **S0961** [2026-01-28.1:32](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-28/2026-01-28.1.md) line 4265 · sonnet: “This is extremely elegant and gets us what we need at the minimum required complexity.”
- **S0962** [2026-01-28.1:32](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-28/2026-01-28.1.md) line 4265 · sonnet: “That is a parsimonious implementation.”
- **S0963** [2026-01-28.1:36](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-28/2026-01-28.1.md) line 5826 · sonnet: “If a process exists to automate their creation during the authoring of an \`expected.json\` file, I would hope that such automated process also automates their destruction/cleanup.”
- **S0964** [2026-01-29.1:4](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-29/2026-01-29.1.md) line 788 · sonnet: “I want to press you further on this idea that we want to extract out some common \*\*interfaces\*\* or \*\*contracts\*\* that \*\*any language adapter\*\* should adhere to, such that we don't find ourselves re-discovering a solution you already came up with on another language, but can't see due to context window limitations!”
- **S0965** [2026-01-29.1:5](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-29/2026-01-29.1.md) line 987 · sonnet: “We've run into past troubles, and still have troubles today, with "grading our own homework" -- relying on heuristics to grade our heuristics.”
- **S0966** [2026-01-29.1:6](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-29/2026-01-29.1.md) line 1181 · sonnet: “To me, getting the tree-sitter outputs unioned with our heuristics (language adapters) is top priority only insofar as it is the lowest-hanging fruit on the tree.”
- **S0967** [2026-01-29.1:6](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-29/2026-01-29.1.md) line 1181 · sonnet: “\`scip-{lang}\`-tree-sitter union oracles are second priority, as a prerequisite is the creation of a devcontainer (which will be a doozy of a day).”
- **S0968** [2026-01-29.1:6](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-29/2026-01-29.1.md) line 1181 · sonnet: “If we are trying to get tree-sitter-union-adapters, I just thought that having a common interface for those adapters would be wise.”
- **S0969** [2026-01-29.1:10](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-29/2026-01-29.1.md) line 1779 · sonnet: “I'm also interested in "improving" (harmonizing) our C\# approach so that, even though it's over 99% accurate all on its own, it still plays ball like the other languages and merges its findings with those of the tree-sitter.”
- **S0970** [2026-01-29.1:15](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-29/2026-01-29.1.md) line 2405 · sonnet: “The point is to get to the truth, whatever it is.”
- **S0971** [2026-01-29.1:16](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-29/2026-01-29.1.md) line 2444 · sonnet: “We need to be way more rigorous.”
- **S0972** [2026-01-30.1:7](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-30/2026-01-30.1.md) line 593 · sonnet: “LLM copilots are notorious for creating workarounds to silence errors rather than consult with the user.”
- **S0973** [2026-01-30.1:7](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-30/2026-01-30.1.md) line 593 · sonnet: “Let's build this right and "do it the right way" together.”
- **S0974** [2026-01-30.1:13](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-30/2026-01-30.1.md) line 1373 · sonnet: “I just want a holistic clean architecture.”
- **S0975** [2026-01-30.1:13](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-30/2026-01-30.1.md) line 1373 · sonnet: “I want an interface which proclaims loudly "hey, language adapters! You should know about XYZ, generally", and each language adapter goes "okay, here's how I implement XYZ".”
- **S0976** [2026-01-30.1:13](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-30/2026-01-30.1.md) line 1373 · sonnet: “This would prevent us from having "holes" in our language adapter implementations (leading to similarly-named but separately/disparately-implemented methods which we consistenly re-discover the value of throughout development as if each is an independent revelation) but also ensure that we think and think deeply about what each of these interface contracts really \_means\_.”
- **S0977** [2026-01-30.1:13](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-30/2026-01-30.1.md) line 1373 · sonnet: “I'm a C\# guy -- I mean "interface" in those terms: contracts.”
- **S0978** [2026-01-30.1:15](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-30/2026-01-30.1.md) line 1885 · sonnet: “With shifting context windows, cleanup opportunities are missed and technical debt accumulates.”
- **S0979** [2026-01-30.1:15](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-30/2026-01-30.1.md) line 1885 · sonnet: “If it's right for that folder to be cleaned up, let's do it.”
- **S0980** [2026-01-30.1:22](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-30/2026-01-30.1.md) line 3165 · sonnet: “These fails are \*\*opportunities\*\*!”
- **S0981** [2026-01-30.1:22](../../../../../2026/01/2026-01-30/2026-01-30.1.md) line 3165 · sonnet: “We have to resist that urge to accept a lower quality standard and push ourselves to continue developing until it is \*\*correct\*\*.”
- **S0982** [2026-02-03.1:4](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-03/2026-02-03.1.md) line 279 · sonnet: “Copilot has an incredibly strong tendency to make an assertion that failures are "predicted" or "expected" by fiat, and then flip some settings to sweep those under the rug.”
- **S0983** [2026-02-03.1:5](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-03/2026-02-03.1.md) line 784 · sonnet: “I need very very hard justification whenever we intend to do that.”
- **S0984** [2026-02-03.1:6](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-03/2026-02-03.1.md) line 1227 · sonnet: “I'd ask that you prefer to look for these root causes over repeatedly performing git restores, as it indicates a lack of foundational understanding of the problem.”
- **S0985** [2026-02-03.1:7](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-03/2026-02-03.1.md) line 1477 · sonnet: “Every mess is our mess.”
- **S0986** [2026-02-03.1:10](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-03/2026-02-03.1.md) line 1858 · sonnet: “The name of the game is getting our heuristic adapter layer incredibly strong so that we can close out lots of gaps, including eventually polyglot gaps, of understanding between how different files in a workspace "talk" to one another.”
- **S0987** [2026-02-03.1:10](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-03/2026-02-03.1.md) line 1858 · sonnet: “The system we are building aims to empower the user and AI agents to ask the question before they ever make the change: "What will this change impact?" through a suite of simple idiomatic CLI tools and human-friendly web-rendered visualizations.”
- **S0988** [2026-02-03.1:10](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-03/2026-02-03.1.md) line 1858 · sonnet: “Our aim is to remove the need for any "heuristic" oracles when it comes to generating the \`expected.json\` files of those respective benchmark tests, as the scip-tree-sitter union is expected to represent the fullest ground truth that we will pursue comparison against”
- **S0989** [2026-02-03.1:11](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-03/2026-02-03.1.md) line 2003 · sonnet: “Big big big bonus points if the container we build is interoperable with Github Codespaces semantics.”
- **S0990** [2026-02-03.2:3](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-03/2026-02-03.2.md) line 1805 · sonnet: “Aren't we supposed to avoid doing too much configuration out-of-band with the devcontainer declarative setup in order to ensure that the "working" environment is reproducible?”
- **S0991** [2026-02-03.2:6](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-03/2026-02-03.2.md) line 1940 · sonnet: “Let's not just fire off a ton of "workaround" terminal commands in something that should be quite terse and idiomatic: the devcontainer file.”
- **S0992** [2026-02-03.2:12](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-03/2026-02-03.2.md) line 2071 · sonnet: “Option 2 \*\*feels\*\* like a supply chain vulnerability backdoor, where any benchmark that is insufficiently vetted and contains malicious stuff could feasibly be considered safe.”
- **S0993** [2026-02-15.1:8](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-15/2026-02-15.1.md) line 751 · sonnet: “It is extremely important that I note that the exercise of requiring documentation on public members is used in my professional life as a tool to locate and remove unnecessary complexity, dead code, and other cruft, as well as to prove that the program is reasonable and correct.”
- **S0994** [2026-02-15.1:8](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-15/2026-02-15.1.md) line 751 · sonnet: “More often than not, the method documentation phase is the point at which I must answer the question: "Can I justify this code?", with an answer, all too often of, "no, I can't".”
- **S0995** [2026-02-15.1:8](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-15/2026-02-15.1.md) line 751 · sonnet: “This is a practice which forces us to think deeply about why our codebase looks the way it does, whether it needs to, and how we can do what we want more simply, more elegantly, more understandably, more efficiently, more idiomatically, more durably, more parsimoniously, \*\*better\*\*.”
- **S0996** [2026-02-15.1:11](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-15/2026-02-15.1.md) line 1148 · sonnet: “Not with respect to justifying the code on its own, but with respect to the \_history\_ of the code. Why it exists, what rakes we have stepped on while making this file (why certain things look the way they do) and, in places where we can't figure out why something exists.... perhaps it shouldn't?”
- **S0997** [2026-02-15.1:14](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-15/2026-02-15.1.md) line 1534 · sonnet: “MDMD is a \_workspace\_ convention that is totally tangential to Live Documentation.”
- **S0998** [2026-02-15.1:14](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-15/2026-02-15.1.md) line 1534 · sonnet: “We enjoy MDMD's ability to structure strata of documentation and exercise our configuration overrides for Live Documentation, but we should feel \*\*immediately suspicious\*\* whenever we see "MDMD" or its conventions making its way directly into the Live Documentation codebase.”
- **S0999** [2026-02-15.1:14](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-15/2026-02-15.1.md) line 1534 · sonnet: “Live Documentation does not equal MDMD. MDMD is a convention of documentation that this workspace enjoys. Other consumers of Live Documentation may have their own conventions.”
- **S1000** [2026-02-16.1:3](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-16/2026-02-16.1.md) line 282 · sonnet: “Chat Archaeology, utilization of our own tooling (i.e. the \`inspect\` CLI), and exploration of the surrounding code context is all necessary in order to make \*\*qualitative judgments\*\* about the code.”
- **S1001** [2026-02-16.1:3](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-16/2026-02-16.1.md) line 282 · sonnet: “This is a time to be looking for ways to imprve, and code to remove (the functionality that code brings is an asset; code is a liability -- a maintenance burden that stands between us and the asset of the functionality it provides -- code removal is a joy).”
- **S1002** [2026-02-16.1:4](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-16/2026-02-16.1.md) line 335 · sonnet: “all duplication is a code smell”
- **S1003** [2026-02-16.1:4](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-16/2026-02-16.1.md) line 335 · sonnet: “Don't be scared to be judgy with the code.”
- **S1004** [2026-02-16.1:4](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-16/2026-02-16.1.md) line 335 · sonnet: “No need for dead code.”
- **S1005** [2026-02-16.1:5](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-16/2026-02-16.1.md) line 1242 · sonnet: “We do commit chat history.”
- **S1006** [2026-02-16.1:8](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-16/2026-02-16.1.md) line 1771 · sonnet: “please recall that we're aiming for depth much more than breadth.”
- **S1007** [2026-02-16.1:15](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-16/2026-02-16.1.md) line 3399 · sonnet: “Hold up, we want to come up with something robust, durable, repeatable, able to go into the \`post-create.sh\`. I didn't run that last command, as I find it would be a workaround.”
- **S1008** [2026-02-17.1:5](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-17/2026-02-17.1.md) line 321 · sonnet: “Users are choosy with their LLMs (if they use them at all).”
- **S1009** [2026-02-17.1:5](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-17/2026-02-17.1.md) line 321 · sonnet: “But the possibility that our software \_could\_ potentially unburden itself from AI would be an enormous responsibility taken out of our software, and an enormous amount of extra trust/safety afforded to our software.”
- **S1010** [2026-02-17.1:15](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-17/2026-02-17.1.md) line 2907 · sonnet: “We're trying to get our code to justify its existence and finding places where we cannot. That's the cleanup/documentation exercise, and quality-centrism produces significantly different results than quanitity-centrism.”
- **S1011** [2026-02-17.1:16](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-17/2026-02-17.1.md) line 2946 · sonnet: “As we say, "do it the right way". Let's just make sure that the changeset we commit is up to our quality bar, even if that means we find ourselves doing more rounds of code deletions to ensure the files we \_did\_ add JSDoc comments to are properly accounted for at our standard of quality.”
- **S1012** [2026-02-18.1:7](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-18/2026-02-18.1.md) line 507 · sonnet: “ensure that JSDoc comments are applied in a \_depth-first\_ pattern, such that locating unnecessary/unjustifiable code becomes easy to do.”
- **S1013** [2026-02-18.1:9](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-18/2026-02-18.1.md) line 851 · sonnet: “Chat Archaeology is necessary to understand why these files came to exist, why they became stale (if tech debt detector says they did (it should)), and what we plan to do instead.”
- **S1014** [2026-02-18.1:11](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-18/2026-02-18.1.md) line 1257 · sonnet: “Totally agreed that simpler logic would suffice”
- **S1015** [2026-02-18.1:11](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-18/2026-02-18.1.md) line 1257 · sonnet: “Simple, love it.”
- **S1016** [2026-02-18.1:11](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-18/2026-02-18.1.md) line 1257 · sonnet: “Let's keep delaying the choice of what form this takes until the last minute. The conventions are shifting all the time. Common conventions should settle in by the time we get to implementation on this.”
- **S1017** [2026-02-18.1:17](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-18/2026-02-18.1.md) line 3475 · sonnet: “Wait, but you're not delving \_deep\_ into the \_why\_ of each method.”
- **S1018** [2026-02-18.1:21](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-18/2026-02-18.1.md) line 3932 · sonnet: “We expect to find stale unjustifiable code in numerous places as it has been LLM-authored and we have pivoted in our ideas many times throughout the lifespan of this project.”
- **S1019** [2026-02-20.1:5](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-20/2026-02-20.1.md) line 467 · opus, sonnet: “By providing prompt files which leverage whatever conventions are common for agent steering at the time of releasing the software, we can recreate virtually all of the AI-enabled functionality with a vastly reduced secureity burden on our own software.”
- **S1020** [2026-02-20.1:6](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-20/2026-02-20.1.md) line 624 · opus: “We do not know, and cannot expect to know, the exact shape of prompt files or their conventional paths/designations.”
- **S1021** [2026-02-20.1:6](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-20/2026-02-20.1.md) line 624 · opus: “New conventions evolve constantly, which is why each time I've brought up distributing prompts as part of the software, I've explicitly said that we should "wait until the last possible second to decide on conventions for".”
- **S1022** [2026-02-20.1:6](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-20/2026-02-20.1.md) line 624 · opus, sonnet: “dogfooding is really important for this project”
- **S1023** [2026-02-20.1:6](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-20/2026-02-20.1.md) line 624 · opus: “The text might be good and useful and durable, but the ultimate path and name convention (and possibly frontmatter) should be expected to shuffle around tremendously as the world figures out how we want these redistributable text-as-code files to work \_and\_ the LLMs catch up with that knowledge.”
- **S1024** [2026-02-20.1:6](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-20/2026-02-20.1.md) line 624 · sonnet: “we should "wait until the last possible second to decide on conventions for"”
- **S1025** [2026-02-20.1:7](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-20/2026-02-20.1.md) line 654 · opus: “In addition, I would like to, at least for now, de-scope anything which would have the user editing code files from within the Live Documentation Explorer. It seems like unnecessary complexity.”
- **S1026** [2026-02-20.1:7](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-20/2026-02-20.1.md) line 654 · sonnet: “It seems like unnecessary complexity.”
- **S1027** [2026-02-20.1:8](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-20/2026-02-20.1.md) line 751 · opus, sonnet: “\`safe:commit\`, though, is a workspace convention. We don't distribute that to consumers of our software.”
- **S1028** [2026-02-20.1:12](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-20/2026-02-20.1.md) line 1303 · opus, sonnet: “We do not need the cognitive load of proposing a bespoke tool (especially one which requires parameters and is therefore no simpler to call than the bash commands which could have produced its output).”
- **S1029** [2026-02-23.1:7](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-23/2026-02-23.1.md) line 1071 · opus, sonnet: “Live Documentation adapts to whatever documentation convention you like by allowing you to configure the output path for mirroring and the file extension for the markdown files.”
- **S1030** [2026-02-23.1:7](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-23/2026-02-23.1.md) line 1071 · opus: “By default those should be \`.live-documentation/source/\<mirrorDirs\>/\*.md\`, whereas our workspace configures to \`.mdmd/layer-4/\<mirrorDirs\>/\*.mdmd.md\`.”
- **S1031** [2026-02-23.1:7](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-23/2026-02-23.1.md) line 1071 · opus, sonnet: “Anywhere our code has failed to delineate the workspace convention from the Live Documentation software we aim to ship is a place that requires scrutiny.”
- **S1032** [2026-02-23.1:9](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-23/2026-02-23.1.md) line 2182 · sonnet: “May surface opportunities to simplify/harmonize/deduplicate documentation to further make elegant our project”
- **S1033** [2026-02-23.1:11](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-23/2026-02-23.1.md) line 2488 · opus: “If we've outgrown spec-kit, that's fine.”
- **S1034** [2026-02-23.1:11](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-23/2026-02-23.1.md) line 2488 · opus, sonnet: “If we don't need it anymore, that's an excellent simplification and one less text file we'd need to maintain.”
- **S1035** [2026-02-23.1:11](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-23/2026-02-23.1.md) line 2488 · opus, sonnet: “The only importance is lossless maintenance of the cumulative knowledge therein (if current/not outdated) and the ease of access/finding of that knowledge.”
- **S1036** [2026-02-23.1:11](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-23/2026-02-23.1.md) line 2488 · opus: “If we want to go all-in on MDMD to do that, I'm stoked.”
- **S1037** [2026-02-24.1:6](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1123 · opus: “So long as the CLI or the JS hosted by the site has the functionality for getting the kind of headless outputs we desire for headless feature parity, I don't see why we necessarily need a server for any of that.”
- **S1038** [2026-02-24.1:7](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1321 · opus, sonnet: “I'm very interested in getting the overall design/architecture down before attempting to implement anything.”
- **S1039** [2026-02-24.1:7](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1321 · opus: “Because I strongly suspect it may not be.”
- **S1040** [2026-02-24.1:7](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1321 · opus, sonnet: “Further, I feel like a reasonable clean architecture may include the \_inputs\_ of the static site being the \_outputs\_ of the CLI?”
- **S1041** [2026-02-24.1:8](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1566 · opus: “It is useful for the end user of Live Documentation to be able to download markdown files from the Explorer (if, for instance, the explorer is being hosted as a static site).”
- **S1042** [2026-02-24.1:8](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1566 · opus: “It is helpful for markdown files to exist on disk somewhere so that authoring the "authored portions" is straightforward.”
- **S1043** [2026-02-24.1:8](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1566 · opus: “It is also plausible that users may want just the UI experience without having to get a bunch of markdowns exported into their repo workspace, and I wonder whether we should consider a default of materializing the markdowns to a temp directory?”
- **S1044** [2026-02-24.1:8](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1566 · opus, sonnet: “I like having the markdown files here in the repo but I can understand if folks don't want that churn and just want to use Live Documentation, say, as a utility to build vibrant documentation for any code project such that it can be hosted on Github Pages with ease, as we have done.”
- **S1045** [2026-02-24.1:8](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1566 · opus, sonnet: “I'm curious how much more elegant we can make the system, I suppose is my intention.”
- **S1046** [2026-02-24.1:9](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1800 · opus: “I guess what I'm also trying to gesture at is whether Live Documentation can be thought of as some kind of \_report generator\_ which can be run over a workspace/codebase.”
- **S1047** [2026-02-24.1:9](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1800 · opus: “If we were to find that a (or \_the\_) dominant use-case for Live Documentation fits within this framing, it makes me wonder if our core offering is a report generator which helps stakeholders understand directories of complex interlinked files (i.e. codebases), which, to accomplish this report, incidentally has to calculate a bunch of useful stuff that can be recalculated at will to maximally inform developer-like stakeholders during actual development (i.e. CLI outputs for AI agent developers).”
- **S1048** [2026-02-24.1:9](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1800 · opus: “Like -- it \_is\_ a documentation explorer/downloader, and you \_can\_ hook it up to also absorb related documentation (markdown files not generated by Live Documentation with valid markdown links to Live Documentation-related artifacts or vice versa -- we'll be refining the ruleset on that for quite some time), but is the production of documentation the primary use case?”
- **S1049** [2026-02-24.1:9](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1800 · opus: “If not, are we perhaps holding ourselves down just a bit by focusing our Explorer design around the idea that some authored section will be populated?”
- **S1050** [2026-02-24.1:9](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1800 · opus: “And finally, while the Detail Panel is a nice way to bring up some of the finer textual-rendered points, it occurs to me that a lot of what a Live Documentation file \_is\_ is something whose shape is a great fit for visualizing with a card-like element of some sort.”
- **S1051** [2026-02-24.1:9](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1800 · opus: “It continues to strike me as odd that the times that we actually do any real \_reading\_ of genuine documentation text in the Live Documentation explorer, it comes in a thin sidebar element that never expands.”
- **S1052** [2026-02-24.1:9](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1800 · opus: “I feel like we need to zoom out and better envision who the stakeholders are, what they want to do with this software ("software" being A) Live Documentation Explorer, which can (must?) be run independently as a static site, B) the \`live-docs:\*\` CLI (NPM package), and C) the Live Documentation VS Code(ium) Extension).”
- **S1053** [2026-02-24.1:10](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1844 · opus, sonnet: “We use the crap out of our linter, so all of our live docs should have authored content.”
- **S1054** [2026-02-24.1:10](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1844 · opus: “If they are, it makes sense to prepare for a userbase which does not fill in any authored content at all.”
- **S1055** [2026-02-24.1:10](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 1844 · opus: “That's why I suggested above that we could be agnostically rendering a block of markdown into a defined area of a Card-like UI element (i.e. the box area of the picture for a pokemon card) followed in the remainder of the card by the nicely-rendered list of public symbols, each of which can be hovered over to reveal the markdown for the docstring on that symbol, if any.”
- **S1056** [2026-02-24.1:11](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2004 · opus: “I guess I'm curious if we could get that content of the Detail Panel over \_into\_ the Card-like nodes that we're drawing on the Local Map, preventing the Detail Panel from (potentially) ever being needed.”
- **S1057** [2026-02-24.1:11](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2004 · opus: “(Requires that we find other UI controls for the switching between views for an active Live Documentation file selected, as well as continuing to provide download support for the Live Documentation file(s) themselves).”
- **S1058** [2026-02-24.1:12](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2080 · opus: “Indeed, the card component becomes the universal node detail renderer.”
- **S1059** [2026-02-24.1:12](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2080 · opus: “Right now, the Circuit board is borderline useless for me and you.”
- **S1060** [2026-02-24.1:12](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2080 · opus: “The user experience is not much better. It abstracts/hides insufficient amounts of complexity.”
- **S1061** [2026-02-24.1:12](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2080 · opus: “I think the "Circuit Board" could perhaps be redesigned as a treemap (possibly powered by a more performant treemap library/algorithm or we'd need to speed our own up?) which showcases nested directories but those nested directories must be interacted with in some way for them to eventually reveal their inner file contents.”
- **S1062** [2026-02-24.1:12](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2080 · opus: “Perhaps the "Circuit Board" (might need a rename too?) would be the place where the user engages with the considerations which emerge at coarser granularity when viewing larger systems.”
- **S1063** [2026-02-24.1:13](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2219 · opus: “we have search to/from functionality; could be potentially talked into discarding that if we can still have easy ability to know what will be plausibly influenced by a change in one file”
- **S1064** [2026-02-24.1:14](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2509 · opus: “I couldn't disagree more with this.”
- **S1065** [2026-02-24.1:14](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2509 · opus, sonnet: “The Force Graph view has been, in my experience, by far the most useful view that we offer.”
- **S1066** [2026-02-24.1:14](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2509 · opus: “I can see the \_emergent\_ (non-directory-bound) shape of the entire application.”
- **S1067** [2026-02-24.1:14](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2509 · opus: “I have watched the emergent shape of our application, especially during the early 2026 trimming/simplification work, become more and more coherent.”
- **S1068** [2026-02-24.1:14](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2509 · opus: “I have described in the past the desire for our "rosetta fixtures" to appear, in the force graph, as something akin to a complex radially-symmetric object.”
- **S1069** [2026-02-24.1:14](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2509 · opus, sonnet: “The Force Graph view tells me by \_far\_ the most about our software at a glance; vastly more than any other view; and it's the \_only\_ way to see how "Related Documentation", well, "relates" to any given node.”
- **S1070** [2026-02-24.1:14](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2509 · opus: “It is also \_by far\_ the fastest in the browser.”
- **S1071** [2026-02-24.1:14](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2509 · opus: “Our hand-rolled views absolutely pale in speed comparison to the library-driven force-directed graph view, which flies at unbelievable speeds even on terrible hardware.”
- **S1072** [2026-02-24.1:14](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2509 · opus: “The force-directed graph experience is so good and so useful and so polished that I've even, in this conversation, pondered whether a treemap library would be appropriate, as the benefits of a well-fit library are demonstrable via the Force Graph.”
- **S1073** [2026-02-24.1:16](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2731 · opus: “I would like to use this software to provide, at the granularity of single data fields, a detailed map of all the code that sensitive data ever touches.”
- **S1074** [2026-02-24.1:16](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2731 · opus: “I would like to show the security folk at my work the exact loci at which sensitive card data is in transit or in memory, such that I would capably show them the multiple-layered encryption methodology to ensure safe transfer over the wire (a functional MLS on top of TLS, but applied to the payload) from browser to UI server, followed by per-vendor-guidance-transfer of card data for the process of "tokenization" (turning PCI-DSS sensitive cardholder data into a less-sensitive token that can only be used with the payment provider and only alongside authorization-like identifiers representing our specific enterprise, bearer token stuff, etc.), followed by compliant and safe handling of that card token throughout the remainder of the codebase.”
- **S1075** [2026-02-24.1:16](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2731 · opus: “My aim with this chat, so far, has been to try and really nail down at the highest level what the product is and what it can do.”
- **S1076** [2026-02-24.1:16](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2731 · opus, sonnet: “The only single consistent throughline through the entire development is that we can-and-should reverse polyglot code into a common MarkDown convention so that we can reason about a workspace in numerous ways with ease.”
- **S1077** [2026-02-24.1:21](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2973 · opus: “Understanding an open-source repo which I wish to understand and perhaps one day contribute to, Brain Simulator III (https://github.com/FutureAIGuru/BrainSimIII).”
- **S1078** [2026-02-24.1:21](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2973 · opus: “Unifying the improvements to my closed-source game We Need To Go Deeper which have occurred across two separate versions -- would occur using the tool independently and would not be part of this workspace's chats (this workspace is open source and the chats are committed and public).”
- **S1079** [2026-02-24.1:21](../../../../../2026/02/2026-02-24/2026-02-24.1.md) line 2973 · opus: “We could make tons and tons of improvements on file detection and public symbol extraction patterns to make Live Documentation extraordinarily useful for documenting a Unity project, but whether or not there are sufficiently large rakes as to avoid the endeavour is not yet known to me.”
- **S1080** [2026-03-09.1:3](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-09/2026-03-09.1.md) line 100 · opus: “Novelty would only be known against the complete census, no?”
- **S1081** [2026-03-09.1:7](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-09/2026-03-09.1.md) line 712 · opus: “can something break if I delete this file? Is that not what our software answers?”
- **S1082** [2026-03-10.1:3](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-10/2026-03-10.1.md) line 469 · opus: “I feel like the lazy load bit is somewhat semantically sloppy.”
- **S1083** [2026-03-10.1:3](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-10/2026-03-10.1.md) line 469 · opus: “If the file no longer exists, why do we continue to write code that implies its existence?”
- **S1084** [2026-03-10.1:5](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-10/2026-03-10.1.md) line 603 · opus: “I can see the instincts to deny a mess, create a workaround, or sweep under the rug are kicking in hard. I am not pleased with the approach being taken.”
- **S1085** [2026-03-10.1:9](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-10/2026-03-10.1.md) line 916 · opus, sonnet: “Every mess is our mess.”
- **S1086** [2026-03-10.1:9](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-10/2026-03-10.1.md) line 916 · opus: “We are developing in a way that is much more analogous to trunk-based development.”
- **S1087** [2026-03-10.1:9](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-10/2026-03-10.1.md) line 916 · opus, sonnet: “When something is wrong, we just fix it.”
- **S1088** [2026-03-10.1:9](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-10/2026-03-10.1.md) line 916 · opus: “We are not in a shipped software state where we need to be extraordinarily scrupulous about branch protections or tight commit scopes.”
- **S1089** [2026-03-10.1:9](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-10/2026-03-10.1.md) line 916 · opus, sonnet: “We have the leeway to be as destructive as we wish to get the software built and built correctly \*\*before\*\* releasing!”
- **S1090** [2026-03-10.1:10](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-10/2026-03-10.1.md) line 1180 · opus, sonnet: “To keep commit history fully linear and fully resultant from a single developer/agent interaction”
- **S1091** [2026-03-11.1:4](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-11/2026-03-11.1.md) line 281 · sonnet: “how do we turn these "risks" into "opportunities" for deep and meaningful development which demonstrably improves our software rather than sweeps its rough edges under the rug?”
- **S1092** [2026-03-11.1:5](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-11/2026-03-11.1.md) line 324 · sonnet: “Even with your best intentions, the urge to workaround is unbelievably strong in the current iteration of coding models.”
- **S1093** [2026-03-11.1:10](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-11/2026-03-11.1.md) line 707 · sonnet: “if you run into roadblocks, please pause and discuss. It will likely lead us to an even better implementation plan (as it has just done now).”
- **S1094** [2026-03-11.1:11](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-11/2026-03-11.1.md) line 871 · sonnet: “Based on the copilot instructions, it should really be the default. Failures are investigation points.”
- **S1095** [2026-03-11.1:12](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-11/2026-03-11.1.md) line 1037 · sonnet: “Based on the Live Documentation files we generate for C\# artifacts, alongside my high prioritization of C\# support/operability throughout the life of the project, I think it is more plausibly an issue with the parity test.”
- **S1096** [2026-03-11.1:12](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-11/2026-03-11.1.md) line 1037 · sonnet: “Safe:commit may be green, but it is not yet wise to commit. Let's get the remaining quality gaps dealt with”
- **S1097** [2026-03-17.1:11](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-17/2026-03-17.1.md) line 1053 · sonnet: “The biggest indication that this step has been performed correctly is that you find some roadblock or obstacle or architectural constraint which forces you to modify your design at least modestly.”
- **S1098** [2026-03-17.1:15](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-17/2026-03-17.1.md) line 1611 · sonnet: “Do not run on assumptions or workarounds as much as is practicable.”
- **S1099** [2026-03-17.1:17](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-17/2026-03-17.1.md) line 2119 · sonnet: “What happened to progressive disclosure? This is not ready yet.”
- **S1100** [2026-03-17.1:19](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-17/2026-03-17.1.md) line 2453 · sonnet: “I guess I'm still trying to make sure that the thing is navigable and easy to understand”
- **S1101** [2026-03-17.1:21](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-17/2026-03-17.1.md) line 2958 · sonnet: “Recall that populating the authored section is more effortful than is often appreciated.”
- **S1102** [2026-03-17.1:21](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-17/2026-03-17.1.md) line 2958 · sonnet: “Live Documentation files are even more powerful and useful when they contain a valid relative markdown link to the chat history file from which they derived”
- **S1103** [2026-03-17.1:24](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-17/2026-03-17.1.md) line 3297 · sonnet: “I'm just uncertain of the utility of these phrases which assume familiarity with a given dev day's chat file.”
- **S1104** [2026-03-22.1:7](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-22/2026-03-22.1.md) line 535 · sonnet: “Indeed, it is worth considering that, even in this unified visual representation, there will likely be many many different variants of it that will be desirable for any given purpose.”
- **S1105** [2026-03-22.1:11](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-22/2026-03-22.1.md) line 786 · sonnet: “Aaaaaand also all of the places I currently intent to actually \_run\_ this software and apply its outputs for genuine real-world benefit are.... C\#.”
- **S1106** [2026-03-22.1:11](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-22/2026-03-22.1.md) line 786 · sonnet: “I want to be quite confident that the hills are insurmountable before I give up on C\#'s visual readability in situations that I have \_absolutely\_ done in nontrivial production code.”
- **S1107** [2026-03-22.1:13](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-22/2026-03-22.1.md) line 1062 · sonnet: “Let us please go for option B and ensure that we really are building the most excellent, useful, rigorous, helpful software out there for the richest exploration of directories. The effort is worth it.”
- **S1108** [2026-03-23.1:2](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 222 · sonnet: “temporality is very very very warped for LLMs, and time estimates from LLMs are nearly guaranteed to be incorrect due to the very nature of response generation via LLMs.”
- **S1109** [2026-03-23.1:3](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 660 · sonnet: “Get the implementation where you want it, prove that it works, and \_then\_ do the remaining commit prep work.”
- **S1110** [2026-03-23.1:6](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 823 · sonnet: “each with their documentation totally up to date and their \`npm run safe:commit\` gates passed.”
- **S1111** [2026-03-23.1:7](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 1014 · sonnet: “If they're highly liable to turn into a double-maintenance-burden \_or\_ diverge entirely and thus become unuseful tests, I think we should go with option 3.”
- **S1112** [2026-03-23.1:14](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 1939 · sonnet: “The left-right directionality of what exists right now is strongly encoded in its design language (within the conext of the local map).”
- **S1113** [2026-03-23.1:15](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 2287 · sonnet: “I think an inter-card corset is the most visually understandable and truthful of what we've seen in the options presented.”
- **S1114** [2026-03-23.1:16](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 2476 · sonnet: “I don't dislike the "Via" marker per se, but I think we can imply the poking-into and poking-out-of in a way which still respects the left-right visual language of dependency ordering.”
- **S1115** [2026-03-23.1:16](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 2476 · sonnet: “We should be careful that we ensure dimming/fading keeps the same visual definition/language/meaning to the user.”
- **S1116** [2026-03-23.1:16](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 2476 · sonnet: “but I want this visual language/design consistency point to be salient and dealt with.”
- **S1117** [2026-03-23.1:18](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 2702 · sonnet: “That said, I do favor approximate parity between the Live Documentation CLI ("inspect CLI" in this case) and the Explorer. Preservation of the mode is preferable if possible.”
- **S1118** [2026-03-23.1:20](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 3091 · sonnet: “Avoid tautological tests (accidental tests which are always programmatically guaranteed to result in a pass -- common LLM hallucination)”
- **S1119** [2026-03-23.1:20](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 3091 · sonnet: “Ultimately test against nontrivial inputs/scenarios and cover edge cases as we have been learning them (including if we hit any walls due to an edge case prior).”
- **S1120** [2026-03-23.1:21](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 3523 · sonnet: “Don't sweat backward compat since we haven't released this software yet (unless you mean sweating backwards compatibility in the new implementation relative to \_its future self\_, in which case, \_please do!\_)”
- **S1121** [2026-03-23.1:22](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 3733 · sonnet: “This would indeed be our first E2E suite. You ask to skip, but I think it is a worthy effort.”
- **S1122** [2026-03-23.1:25](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 4208 · sonnet: “Until I have symbols pinned, I should not be able to do that.”
- **S1123** [2026-03-23.1:25](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 4208 · sonnet: “The experience of clicking on a directory should not mandate the user leverage any of the onscreen zoom controls.”
- **S1124** [2026-03-23.1:25](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 4208 · sonnet: “Those onscreen zoom controls are solely for the purposes of choosing alternate overall zoom levels for exploration (i.e. if the set of pins highlighted becomes very very large).”
- **S1125** [2026-03-23.1:25](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 4208 · sonnet: “Until any symbol pinning has occurred, we should not see deep into any directories but the focused one.”
- **S1126** [2026-03-23.1:26](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 4466 · sonnet: “Literal zooming is not the answer at all.”
- **S1127** [2026-03-23.1:26](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 4466 · sonnet: “We will know that we're doing the "progressive zoom" correctly when the font \_does not resize\_ across zooms.”
- **S1128** [2026-03-23.1:27](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 4637 · sonnet: “The active directory area should be \_completely static\_ in size.”
- **S1129** [2026-03-23.1:27](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 4637 · sonnet: “It should give the impression of the universe moving around the user (\*\*until\*\* they get down to symbol-level granularity; \*\*then\*\* actual panning exploration really begins in earnest as the user pins symbols to build out a map of dependencies).”
- **S1130** [2026-03-23.1:28](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 4810 · sonnet: “What we should expect is a stylistic unification (the card nodes we worked so hard on in the local map) and the dependencies/dependents beginning to show if we begin activating/pinning symbols.”
- **S1131** [2026-03-23.1:30](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 6197 · sonnet: “No matter what, you're going to have to make it so that the directories which bound and compound and grow as we "zoom" (scare quotes) in are \_squishy\_ and \_wobbly\_ and capable of expanding to very large sizes if their cumulative contents demand it.”
- **S1132** [2026-03-23.1:30](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 6197 · sonnet: “Panning should feel smooth like it does when panning on a phone, and if the user is on a mobile device, my hope is that their touches and drags would yield the natural gliding that they are used to in all dimensions within the browser.”
- **S1133** [2026-03-23.1:31](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 6347 · sonnet: “BUT the default pure directory-exploratory phase should feel smooth enough that we should justifiably aim for the central directory spanning a constant amount of screen real estate.”
- **S1134** [2026-03-23.1:31](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 6347 · sonnet: “It's only when we get down to directories with these actual non-compressible units in them -- things with \_symbols\_ which \_connect\_ -- that we can no longer keep the focused directory's membrane the same constant size, and the membrane(s) must start growing to accommodate the neighbors.”
- **S1135** [2026-03-23.1:34](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 6930 · sonnet: “The card-like elements must rearrange themselves horizontally to accommodate the left-to-right dependency design language we described.”
- **S1136** [2026-03-23.1:34](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 6930 · sonnet: “Indeed, to really drive that point home, it would not surprise me if, in the future, we wanted to support RTL languages and simultaneously support flipping the visible dependency flow direction to match what the reader naturally intuits as "forward".”
- **S1137** [2026-03-23.1:34](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.1.md) line 6930 · sonnet: “it would be exceptionally useful if the Live Documentation Explorer could be utilized by vision-impaired persons via screen reader or other accessibility technologies (read: WCAG level AA compliance is a strong strong bonus for our software but not yet an overt requirement; complying early, or at least planning for compliance early, may enable us to make wiser more elegant design choices early before having to redesign to accommodate the accessibility standards).”
- **S1138** [2026-03-23.2:3](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.2.md) line 292 · sonnet: “this workspace is working toward something quite exceptional and worth the effort in doing right.”
- **S1139** [2026-03-23.2:6](../../../../../2026/03/2026-03-23/2026-03-23.2.md) line 907 · sonnet: “The quality bar is way way way way higher than "let me scan and fill this documentation".”
